- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Synthetic Data is Sufficient for Zero-Shot Visual Generalization from Offline Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12356v1)
- **Authors**: Ahmet H. Güzel, Ilija Bogunovic, Jack Parker-Holder
- **Abstract**: Offline reinforcement learning (RL) offers a promising framework for training agents using pre-collected datasets without the need for further environment interaction. However, policies trained on offline data often struggle to generalise due to limited exposure to diverse states. The complexity of visual data introduces additional challenges such as noise, distractions, and spurious correlations, which can misguide the policy and increase the risk of overfitting if the training data is not sufficiently diverse. Indeed, this makes it challenging to leverage vision-based offline data in training robust agents that can generalize to unseen environments. To solve this problem, we propose a simple approach generating additional synthetic training data. We propose a two-step process, first augmenting the originally collected offline data to improve zero-shot generalization by introducing diversity, then using a diffusion model to generate additional data in latent space. We test our method across both continuous action spaces (Visual D4RL) and discrete action spaces (Procgen), demonstrating that it significantly improves generalization without requiring any algorithmic changes to existing model-free offline RL methods. We show that our method not only increases the diversity of the training data but also significantly reduces the generalization gap at test time while maintaining computational efficiency. We believe this approach could fuel additional progress in generating synthetic data to train more general agents in the future.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a two-step method to improve zero-shot generalization in offline reinforcement learning (RL) from visual inputs.  The approach combines data augmentation with diffusion model-based upsampling. First, data augmentation techniques (rotation, color jitter, color cutout, background overlay) are applied to the offline dataset to increase diversity and reduce overfitting. Second, a diffusion model is used to upsample the augmented dataset in the latent space, generating synthetic data points that capture potentially unseen transitions. The method is evaluated on Visual D4RL (continuous action) and Procgen (discrete action) benchmarks, demonstrating improved generalization performance without requiring algorithmic changes to existing model-free offline RL methods. The results suggest that this approach increases training data diversity and reduces the generalization gap at test time.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of data augmentation and diffusion model-based upsampling specifically for *offline* RL with *visual inputs*. While both data augmentation and diffusion models have been used separately in online RL and generative tasks, adapting and combining them to address the unique challenges of offline visual RL is a worthwhile contribution. The use of a latent space diffusion model is a practical choice for reducing computational costs. The application to *both* continuous and discrete action spaces increases the breadth and impact of the work. Furthermore, the application of fixed distracting data to further improve generalization adds an interesting element, although it could be considered an extension of the core idea.

*   **Significance:** Improving generalization in offline RL is a critical challenge, particularly when dealing with visual inputs which are susceptible to spurious correlations. The paper offers a relatively simple and computationally efficient solution that can be readily integrated with existing model-free offline RL algorithms. The empirical results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method across different environments and benchmarks. The method has the potential to make offline RL more practical in real-world scenarios where data is pre-collected and generalizing to new environments is essential. However, the reliance on benchmark datasets, and particularly the relatively small number of levels in the Procgen dataset used, somewhat limits the significance of the findings in terms of their applicability to completely novel, real-world scenarios. The authors acknowledge limitations regarding model complexity in the long run.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-defined and relatively simple approach.
    *   Comprehensive empirical evaluation on relevant benchmarks (V-D4RL and Procgen).
    *   Demonstrated improved generalization performance.
    *   Computational efficiency (due to latent space diffusion model).
    *   Addresses a crucial challenge in offline RL.
    *   The JS Divergence analysis provides further insights into the distribution alignment.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The core idea is an *integration* of existing techniques (data augmentation and diffusion models). While the specific application to offline visual RL is novel, the individual components are not.
    *   The improvements are significant but not revolutionary. It addresses a known weakness and shows how to significantly reduce it.
    *   Limited experimentation on real-world environments. Benchmarks are controlled, and may not fully capture the complexities of real-world visual data.
    *   The limitations regarding increasing the number of levels in the Procgen dataset and its impact on generalization performance should be highlighted.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging the use of data augmentation and diffusion models for offline visual RL. The simplicity and effectiveness of the approach could lead to its adoption by other researchers and practitioners. The insights into the importance of data diversity and the limitations of purely algorithmic solutions could also inform future research directions.

*   **Rigour:** The experimental section is well designed and described thoroughly, demonstrating that the approach is robust and reliable.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a valuable contribution, its novelty is somewhat incremental. It cleverly combines existing techniques for a specific problem (offline visual RL). The empirical results are strong, and the method addresses a practical challenge, but the limited exploration in real-world environments and incremental nature of the approach justifies a score in the higher-mid range. The work serves as an excellent starting point for further research on improving generalization in offline visual RL.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Uncovering Systematic Failures of LLMs in Verifying Code Against Natural Language Specifications
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12358v1)
- **Authors**: Haolin Jin, Huaming Chen
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have become essential tools in software development, widely used for requirements engineering, code generation and review tasks. Software engineers often rely on LLMs to assess whether system code implementation satisfy task requirements, thereby enhancing code robustness and accuracy. However, it remains unclear whether LLMs can reliably determine whether the code complies fully with the given task descriptions, which is usually natural language specifications. In this paper, we uncover a systematic failure of LLMs in evaluating whether code aligns with natural language requirements. Specifically, with widely used benchmarks, we employ unified prompts to judge code correctness. Our results reveal that LLMs frequently misclassify correct code implementations as either ``not satisfying requirements'' or containing potential defects. Surprisingly, more complex prompting, especially when leveraging prompt engineering techniques involving explanations and proposed corrections, leads to higher misjudgment rate, which highlights the critical reliability issues in using LLMs as code review assistants. We further analyze the root causes of these misjudgments, and propose two improved prompting strategies for mitigation. For the first time, our findings reveals unrecognized limitations in LLMs to match code with requirements. We also offer novel insights and practical guidance for effective use of LLMs in automated code review and task-oriented agent scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to verify code against natural language specifications.  The authors uncover a systematic failure where LLMs frequently misclassify correct code implementations as incorrect, meaning they don't satisfy the requirements. Surprisingly, more complex prompts involving explanations and proposed corrections lead to *higher* misjudgment rates. The authors analyze the root causes, attributing them to an "over-correction bias" where LLMs tend to assume flaws even when the code is correct. They propose improved prompting strategies (Two-Phase Reflective Prompt and Behavioral Comparison Prompt) to mitigate this bias, which show promising results. The study highlights limitations of LLMs in matching code with requirements, offering guidance for using LLMs in automated code review.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The identification of LLMs' systematic failure to accurately verify code against natural language specifications, particularly the *increased* error rate with more complex prompts, is a novel and somewhat counterintuitive finding. This challenges the common assumption that chain-of-thought prompting always enhances performance. The proposal of the "over-correction bias" and subsequent mitigation strategies represents a contribution to understanding LLM behavior.

*   **Significance:** The work has significant implications for the use of LLMs in software engineering.  If LLMs are prone to false negatives in code verification, their practical utility as automated code review assistants is seriously compromised. The finding is crucial for developers and researchers working on LLM-based software development tools. The proposed mitigation strategies are also valuable contributions that have potential for immediate improvements in applications that leverage LLMs for code review and verification.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of LLM misjudgment in code verification.
    *   **Empirical Evidence:** The study uses standard code evaluation benchmarks and multiple LLMs to provide strong empirical support for its claims.
    *   **Thorough Analysis:** The authors provide a thorough analysis of the root causes, identifying the "over-correction bias."
    *   **Practical Solutions:** The paper offers practical solutions in the form of improved prompting strategies.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors state they will release a full replication package, enhancing the reproducibility of the study.
    *   **Addressing a Critical Problem:** The work addresses a critical problem: LLMs are being increasingly adopted as code review assistants, therefore, understanding their systematic failure to align code to requirements is crucial.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Mitigation:** While the proposed mitigation strategies show improvement, the RCRR values still leave room for improvement, and may be very dependent on the type of code.
    *   **Benchmark Dependence:**  The findings are based on specific code evaluation benchmarks and LLMs. Further research is needed to assess the generalizability of the results across different types of code, programming languages, and LLM architectures.
    *   **Reliance on Python:** As stated by the authors, Python is their main programming language, which limits generalizability to other languages.

*   **Impact:** This paper has the potential to significantly influence how LLMs are used in software engineering. It is a crucial cautionary tale against over-reliance on LLMs for code verification, emphasizing the need for careful prompt engineering and validation. Further research will likely build upon these findings to develop more reliable LLM-based code review tools. The work will spur more targeted analysis of LLM biases in code-related tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant finding about the limitations of LLMs in code verification. The empirical evidence is strong, and the analysis is thorough.  The proposed mitigation strategies are a valuable starting point for future research.  While the study has some limitations in scope and generalizability, the core finding is important and has the potential to change the way LLMs are used in software development. Due to the paper's significant implications for practical LLM deployments in software engineering, but also taking into consideration the limited scope of mitigation and generalizability, the paper receives a score of 8.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Navigating the Exploration-Exploitation Tradeoff in Inference-Time Scaling of Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12361v1)
- **Authors**: Xun Su, Jianming Huang, Yang Yusen, Zhongxi Fang, Hiroyuki Kasai
- **Abstract**: Inference-time scaling has achieved remarkable success in language models, yet its adaptation to diffusion models remains underexplored. We observe that the efficacy of recent Sequential Monte Carlo (SMC)-based methods largely stems from globally fitting the The reward-tilted distribution, which inherently preserves diversity during multi-modal search. However, current applications of SMC to diffusion models face a fundamental dilemma: early-stage noise samples offer high potential for improvement but are difficult to evaluate accurately, whereas late-stage samples can be reliably assessed but are largely irreversible. To address this exploration-exploitation trade-off, we approach the problem from the perspective of the search algorithm and propose two strategies: Funnel Schedule and Adaptive Temperature. These simple yet effective methods are tailored to the unique generation dynamics and phase-transition behavior of diffusion models. By progressively reducing the number of maintained particles and down-weighting the influence of early-stage rewards, our methods significantly enhance sample quality without increasing the total number of Noise Function Evaluations. Experimental results on multiple benchmarks and state-of-the-art text-to-image diffusion models demonstrate that our approach outperforms previous baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of inference-time scaling in diffusion models, drawing parallels to successful techniques in large language models.  It identifies an exploration-exploitation trade-off: early-stage noise samples are hard to evaluate but malleable, while late-stage samples are easier to evaluate but less modifiable.  To address this, the authors propose two strategies: (1) **Funnel Schedule (F-SMC)**, which dynamically adjusts the number of particles maintained throughout the sampling process, allocating more particles early on and progressively reducing them, and (2) **Adaptive Temperature (SMC-A)**, which modulates the temperature parameter in the SMC potential function to down-weight early, less reliable reward estimates.  The methods aim to improve sample quality without increasing the total computational budget (NFE).  Experiments across various diffusion models (Stable Diffusion and SDXL) and evaluation metrics (ImageReward, HPSv2, GenEval) demonstrate that the proposed approach, particularly F-SMC-A, outperforms existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies in explicitly framing inference-time scaling as an exploration-exploitation problem within diffusion models, an aspect not heavily emphasized in prior work. The proposed solutions, F-SMC and Adaptive Temperature, are relatively simple to implement but address a fundamental issue. Adaptive Temperature in particular is clever in its adaptive reduction in inaccurate early state reward signals. While techniques like dynamic particle allocation exist in SMC literature, their specific adaptation to the phase transition behavior of diffusion models during image generation is novel. The application of the Tweedie formula to enhance reward estimation is a nice touch.

* **Significance:**

The paper's significance is moderate.  Inference-time scaling for diffusion models is an active research area with many proposed techniques. The paper convincingly demonstrates improvements over standard SMC-based approaches, which represents a substantial step. The insight regarding early-stage vs. late-stage reward estimation is important and could influence future research in this area. Also their analysis of funnel schedule and resampling is important.

* **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Framing:**  Clearly articulates the exploration-exploitation dilemma.
    *   **Simplicity and Effectiveness:** Presents straightforward, implementable solutions with demonstrated performance gains.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** Evaluates across multiple models and metrics, increasing confidence in the generalizability of the approach.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:**  Provides a convergence analysis.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Provide further insight into their model.

* **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While effective, the improvements, while good, aren't game changing.
    *   **Reward Model Dependency:** The method relies on a reward model which can have significant influence on performance. While acknowledging this is important, more discussion on how to make reward models better is needed.
    *   **Clarity:** Some of the descriptions, especially around the theoretical justifications, could be more accessible to a broader audience.

* **Potential Influence:**

The paper is likely to have a moderate impact.  It can influence future research by:
    * Guiding researchers to think about inference scaling of DMs as an exploration exploitation problem
    * Inspiring better reward models to mitigate inaccuracies.
    * Contributing to better overall fine-tuned image generation

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7** to this paper.

*Rationale:* The paper addresses an important problem with a novel perspective and provides effective, theoretically sound solutions. It demonstrates clear improvements over existing SMC-based methods. However, the results aren't revolutionary and the reliance on reward models remains a limitation. Therefore, I would not rank it as exceptional. However, it contributes meaningful insights and advancements to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 0/10

### TaoSR1: The Thinking Model for E-commerce Relevance Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12365v1)
- **Authors**: Chenhe Dong, Shaowei Yao, Pengkun Jiao, Jianhui Yang, Yiming Jin, Zerui Huang, Xiaojiang Zhou, Dan Ou, Haihong Tang
- **Abstract**: Query-product relevance prediction is a core task in e-commerce search. BERT-based models excel at semantic matching but lack complex reasoning capabilities. While Large Language Models (LLMs) are explored, most still use discriminative fine-tuning or distill to smaller models for deployment. We propose a framework to directly deploy LLMs for this task, addressing key challenges: Chain-of-Thought (CoT) error accumulation, discriminative hallucination, and deployment feasibility. Our framework, TaoSR1, involves three stages: (1) Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) with CoT to instill reasoning; (2) Offline sampling with a pass@N strategy and Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to improve generation quality; and (3) Difficulty-based dynamic sampling with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) to mitigate discriminative hallucination. Additionally, post-CoT processing and a cumulative probability-based partitioning method enable efficient online deployment. TaoSR1 significantly outperforms baselines on offline datasets and achieves substantial gains in online side-by-side human evaluations, introducing a novel paradigm for applying CoT reasoning to relevance classification.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "TaoSR1: The Thinking Model for E-commerce Relevance Search" introduces a novel framework for optimizing Large Language Models (LLMs) for e-commerce relevance ranking.  The proposed framework aims to address limitations of BERT-based models in handling complex queries and direct LLMs to be used online without distillation. The framework consists of three stages: 1) Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) with Chain-of-Thought (CoT) to enhance reasoning capabilities, utilizing Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) for CoT sample creation; 2) Offline pass@N sampling combined with Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to improve generation quality; and 3) Difficulty-based dynamic sampling integrated with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) to mitigate discriminative hallucination.  A cumulative probability tiering is then used for assigning relevance levels. Experiments demonstrate significant performance improvements compared to baselines in offline evaluation datasets and online A/B testing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its integrated approach to deploying large language models directly in an e-commerce search environment.  While SFT with CoT, DPO, and GRPO have been used individually in other contexts, their combination, specifically tailored to the challenges of e-commerce relevance ranking, and particularly using a continuous valued probability with cumulative probability tiering represents a significant advance. This is further augmented by the focus on reasoning capabilities (using CoT) and the explicit handling of the discriminative hallucinations is also new. The RAG based construction of CoT samples using business rules knowledge base add another layer of novelty. However, there is a lot of overlap with the techniques that are currently in the market.
*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from the potential impact on e-commerce search engines. The paper addresses real-world challenges like deployment latency, error accumulation in CoT, and discriminative hallucination, which are significant barriers to using LLMs in production systems.  The reported improvements in both offline and online evaluations suggest a substantial enhancement in relevance ranking, potentially leading to improved user experience, increased conversion rates, and enhanced merchant satisfaction. If the approach is generally applicable and scalable, it can influence how e-commerce platforms leverage LLMs.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The three-stage optimization pipeline comprehensively tackles key challenges in applying LLMs to e-commerce search.
    *   **Practical Focus:** The paper addresses practical deployment issues like latency and error accumulation, making the proposed framework more relevant to real-world applications.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The experimental results demonstrate substantial improvements over existing methods, both in offline evaluations and online A/B testing.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of traditional approaches and motivates the need for a reasoning-based framework.
    *   **The Cumulative Probability Tiering (CumPT)** approach offers a simplification in online deployment, requiring only a single hyperparameter, a notable practical advantage.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Closed-Source Foundation Model:**  The dependency on Tbstar, a closed-source LLM makes it more difficult to reproduce results and restricts applicability of the methods to other platforms without access to such a model.
    *   **Limited Generalizability Evidence:** While Taobao is a large platform, the specific business rules and data characteristics may not be directly transferable to other e-commerce platforms. The paper would benefit from discussions on the potential need for adaptation across different settings.
    *   **Limited Details:** The exact implementation details of the RAG retrieval system and the nature of the knowledge base rules is limited.
    *   **Incremental Improvements**: The individual components, while thoughtfully combined and optimized, are not groundbreaking innovations in themselves.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach can potentially shift the paradigm for relevance ranking in e-commerce, encouraging further research on reasoning-based models and direct deployment of LLMs. It may also inspire the development of more sophisticated methods for mitigating hallucination and error accumulation.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the above points, the paper offers a noteworthy contribution with the proposed framework. It combines existing techniques in a novel manner to solve a practical, real-world problem, achieving impressive performance improvements. The focus on deployability and handling specific LLM failure modes adds to its value. However, the use of a closed-source model and the incremental nature of many of the components prevents a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### GraphCogent: Overcoming LLMs' Working Memory Constraints via Multi-Agent Collaboration in Complex Graph Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12379v1)
- **Authors**: Rongzheng Wang, Qizhi Chen, Yihong Huang, Yizhuo Ma, Muquan Li, Jiakai Li, Ke Qin, Guangchun Luo, Shuang Liang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) show promising performance on small-scale graph reasoning tasks but fail when handling real-world graphs with complex queries. This phenomenon stems from LLMs' inability to effectively process complex graph topology and perform multi-step reasoning simultaneously. To address these limitations, we propose GraphCogent, a collaborative agent framework inspired by human Working Memory Model that decomposes graph reasoning into specialized cognitive processes: sense, buffer, and execute. The framework consists of three modules: Sensory Module standardizes diverse graph text representations via subgraph sampling, Buffer Module integrates and indexes graph data across multiple formats, and Execution Module combines tool calling and model generation for efficient reasoning. We also introduce Graph4real, a comprehensive benchmark contains with four domains of real-world graphs (Web, Social, Transportation, and Citation) to evaluate LLMs' graph reasoning capabilities. Our Graph4real covers 21 different graph reasoning tasks, categorized into three types (Structural Querying, Algorithmic Reasoning, and Predictive Modeling tasks), with graph scales that are 10 times larger than existing benchmarks. Experiments show that Llama3.1-8B based GraphCogent achieves a 50% improvement over massive-scale LLMs like DeepSeek-R1 (671B). Compared to state-of-the-art agent-based baseline, our framework outperforms by 20% in accuracy while reducing token usage by 80% for in-toolset tasks and 30% for out-toolset tasks. Code will be available after review.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GraphCogent: Overcoming LLMs' Working Memory Constraints via Multi-Agent Collaboration in Complex Graph Understanding":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of Large Language Models (LLMs) in handling complex graph reasoning tasks due to their working memory constraints.  It proposes GraphCogent, a collaborative agent framework inspired by the human working memory model. GraphCogent decomposes the graph reasoning process into three modules: a Sensory Module for standardizing graph text representations via subgraph sampling, a Buffer Module for integrating and indexing graph data across multiple formats, and an Execution Module that combines tool calling and model generation for efficient reasoning.  The paper also introduces Graph4real, a new benchmark dataset of real-world graphs spanning web, social, transportation, and citation domains, designed to evaluate LLMs' graph reasoning capabilities.  Experiments show that GraphCogent, using a Llama3.1-8B base, achieves significant performance improvements over other methods, especially massive-scale LLMs like DeepSeek-R1. The framework also demonstrates token usage reduction compared to agent-based baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits good novelty. The core idea of a multi-agent system mimicking human working memory is conceptually sound and relevant to the challenges LLMs face with complex graph tasks. The decomposition into sensory, buffer, and execution modules is a sensible architectural choice. The innovative combined strategy of using tool calling when possible and falling back on custom model generation when needed is a key contribution. The creation of the Graph4real benchmark is also a significant contribution, as it addresses the lack of real-world, large-scale graph datasets for evaluating LLMs.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is moderate to high. Overcoming the working memory bottleneck in LLMs for graph reasoning is a crucial step towards enabling these models to tackle real-world problems involving complex, interconnected data. The performance gains demonstrated by GraphCogent, especially on the new, challenging Graph4real benchmark, suggests that the proposed approach is promising. Token usage reduction is also valuable, as it makes the framework more efficient and cost-effective.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-Defined Problem:** The paper clearly identifies a critical limitation of LLMs in graph reasoning and provides a compelling motivation for addressing it.
    *   **Sound Architecture:** The proposed architecture is well-reasoned and grounded in cognitive science principles.  The modular design promotes flexibility and maintainability.
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** Graph4real is a valuable contribution to the field, providing a more realistic and challenging testbed for LLM-based graph reasoning methods.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate that GraphCogent achieves state-of-the-art performance and outperforms existing methods on a variety of tasks and graph scales.
    *   **Token usage improvements:** The paper demonstrates impressive token usage reductions, making the framework more practical.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Model Exploration:** The experimental evaluation focuses primarily on one base LLM (Llama3.1-8B). While cross-dataset validation and adaptability is touched upon, more extensive evaluation across different model families would further strengthen the results.
    *   **Scalability details lacking:** While the paper mentions scalability, there isn't a deep dive into the performance bottlenecks that might emerge on extremely large graphs. The framework depends on subgraph sampling but how it adapts for larger graphs isn't thoroughly addressed.
    *   **Code Availability pending:** The lack of readily available code makes it difficult to independently verify the results and reproduce the experiments, limiting the immediate impact of the work.

**Potential Influence:**

This paper has the potential to influence future research in several ways:

*   **Spurring Further Research on Memory-Augmented LLMs:** It could encourage other researchers to explore different memory augmentation techniques for LLMs in various domains.
*   **Adoption of Graph4real:** Graph4real could become a standard benchmark for evaluating graph reasoning capabilities of LLMs, leading to more rigorous and comparable results across different studies.
*   **Development of More Efficient Graph Reasoning Frameworks:** The modular design of GraphCogent could inspire the development of more efficient and scalable graph reasoning frameworks.

**Conclusion:**

GraphCogent presents a novel and effective approach to address the working memory constraints of LLMs in graph reasoning. The paper is well-written, clearly motivated, and supported by strong experimental results. The creation of the Graph4real benchmark further enhances the paper's contribution. The limited model exploration and missing code limits its immediate influence but is definitely an important work for graph based reasoning with LLMs.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates significant novelty and presents strong empirical results within the domain of graph reasoning with LLMs. The ideas presented are well-justified. However, the limited scope and pending code release warrant the score.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### ViT-EnsembleAttack: Augmenting Ensemble Models for Stronger Adversarial Transferability in Vision Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12384v1)
- **Authors**: Hanwen Cao, Haobo Lu, Xiaosen Wang, Kun He
- **Abstract**: Ensemble-based attacks have been proven to be effective in enhancing adversarial transferability by aggregating the outputs of models with various architectures. However, existing research primarily focuses on refining ensemble weights or optimizing the ensemble path, overlooking the exploration of ensemble models to enhance the transferability of adversarial attacks. To address this gap, we propose applying adversarial augmentation to the surrogate models, aiming to boost overall generalization of ensemble models and reduce the risk of adversarial overfitting. Meanwhile, observing that ensemble Vision Transformers (ViTs) gain less attention, we propose ViT-EnsembleAttack based on the idea of model adversarial augmentation, the first ensemble-based attack method tailored for ViTs to the best of our knowledge. Our approach generates augmented models for each surrogate ViT using three strategies: Multi-head dropping, Attention score scaling, and MLP feature mixing, with the associated parameters optimized by Bayesian optimization. These adversarially augmented models are ensembled to generate adversarial examples. Furthermore, we introduce Automatic Reweighting and Step Size Enlargement modules to boost transferability. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ViT-EnsembleAttack significantly enhances the adversarial transferability of ensemble-based attacks on ViTs, outperforming existing methods by a substantial margin. Code is available at https://github.com/Trustworthy-AI-Group/TransferAttack.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "ViT-EnsembleAttack: Augmenting Ensemble Models for Stronger Adversarial Transferability in Vision Transformers" addresses the challenge of improving the transferability of adversarial attacks against Vision Transformers (ViTs). The authors propose a novel ensemble-based attack method called ViT-EnsembleAttack. Instead of solely focusing on optimizing ensemble weights or paths, as in previous works, this approach centers on adversarially augmenting the surrogate models used in the ensemble. This augmentation is achieved through three strategies tailored to ViT architecture: Multi-head dropping (MHD), Attention score scaling (ASS), and MLP feature mixing (MFM). Bayesian optimization is used to determine the optimal parameters for these augmentation techniques. The augmented models are then ensembled, and the attack is further enhanced by Automatic Reweighting and Step Size Enlargement. Experiments demonstrate significant improvements in adversarial transferability against both ViTs and CNNs compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of adversarially augmenting ensemble models, rather than simply optimizing the ensemble *combination* of fixed models, is relatively novel within the domain of transfer-based adversarial attacks. Designing ViT-specific augmentation strategies (MHD, ASS, MFM) is also a good contribution, though these strategies are inspired by existing data augmentation techniques. The use of Bayesian optimization to tune these augmentation parameters adds a layer of sophistication.

*   **Significance:** The paper presents a significant practical improvement in the transferability of adversarial attacks, particularly against ViTs, which are becoming increasingly important. The experiments convincingly demonstrate that ViT-EnsembleAttack outperforms SOTA ensemble methods. Furthermore, the cross-architecture transferability to CNNs is also notably improved.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear Problem Definition: The paper effectively identifies a gap in existing research (lack of focus on augmenting ensemble models themselves).
    *   Well-Designed Approach: The proposed ViT-EnsembleAttack is well-motivated and thoughtfully designed, leveraging ViT-specific characteristics.
    *   Comprehensive Experiments: The experiments are extensive, covering various ViT and CNN architectures, attack settings, and ablation studies. The results are compelling and clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   Thorough Analysis: The paper provides insightful analyses of the effects of different modules and hyperparameters.
    *   Good Writing: The paper is well-written and easy to understand.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Incremental Innovation: While the idea of augmenting ensemble members is novel in this specific context, the augmentation strategies themselves (masking, scaling, mixing) are inspired by prior data augmentation techniques. A genuinely original augmentation strategy could have increased the paper's impact.
    *   Limited Theoretical Analysis: The paper mainly focuses on empirical results. A deeper theoretical analysis of why adversarial augmentation improves transferability would strengthen the contribution. It could discuss whether or not these new augmentation methods help find a flat-minima region when creating the adversarial examples, which could better generalize to other models.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on adversarial attacks and defenses, particularly in the context of ViTs. The idea of adversarially augmenting models within an ensemble could be adopted and extended in various ways. It also highlights the importance of considering the characteristics of specific model architectures when designing attack strategies.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and effective approach to improving the transferability of adversarial attacks against ViTs by adversarially augmenting the surrogate models in the ensemble. The empirical results are strong and demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods. However, the novelty of the specific augmentation strategies is somewhat limited, and a stronger theoretical underpinning would be beneficial.  The design of the augmentations, specific to ViTs, and the thorough experimentation are solid contributions. While not a groundbreaking theoretical advance, the practical significance and thoroughness of the work justify a score of 7.5. The paper's focus on augmentation, use of Bayesian optimization, and attention to ViT architectures makes this a solid contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ReaLM: Reflection-Enhanced Autonomous Reasoning with Small Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12387v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanfeng Xu, Zehui Dai, Jian Liang, Jiapeng Guan, Guangrun Wang, Liang Lin, Xiaohui Lv
- **Abstract**: Small Language Models (SLMs) are a cost-effective alternative to Large Language Models (LLMs), but often struggle with complex reasoning due to their limited capacity and a tendency to produce mistakes or inconsistent answers during multi-step reasoning. Existing efforts have improved SLM performance, but typically at the cost of one or more of three key aspects: (1) reasoning capability, due to biased supervision that filters out negative reasoning paths and limits learning from errors; (2) autonomy, due to over-reliance on externally generated reasoning signals; and (3) generalization, which suffers when models overfit to teacher-specific patterns. In this paper, we introduce ReaLM, a reinforcement learning framework for robust and self-sufficient reasoning in vertical domains. To enhance reasoning capability, we propose Multi-Route Process Verification (MRPV), which contrasts both positive and negative reasoning paths to extract decisive patterns. To reduce reliance on external guidance and improve autonomy, we introduce Enabling Autonomy via Asymptotic Induction (EAAI), a training strategy that gradually fades external signals. To improve generalization, we apply guided chain-of-thought distillation to encode domain-specific rules and expert knowledge into SLM parameters, making them part of what the model has learned. Extensive experiments on both vertical and general reasoning tasks demonstrate that ReaLM significantly improves SLM performance across aspects (1)-(3) above.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ReaLM: Reflection-Enhanced Autonomous Reasoning with Small Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses limitations in Small Language Models (SLMs) when it comes to complex reasoning tasks. These limitations are categorized as: 1) limited reasoning capability due to biased training data, 2) lack of autonomy due to reliance on external guidance, and 3) poor generalization due to overfitting to teacher models.  The authors introduce ReaLM, a reinforcement learning framework designed to improve SLM reasoning in vertical domains. ReaLM comprises three components: Multi-Route Process Verification (MRPV), which contrasts both positive and negative reasoning paths; Enabling Autonomy via Asymptotic Induction (EAAI), a training strategy that gradually fades external signals; and guided chain-of-thought distillation, which embeds domain-specific rules. The paper presents experiments on vertical and general reasoning tasks demonstrating performance improvements.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addressing a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles a crucial challenge: making SLMs viable alternatives to LLMs for real-world deployments. The focus on reasoning capability, autonomy, and generalization are all essential components for building practical SLM systems.
*   **Well-Defined Components:** The individual components of ReaLM (MRPV, EAAI, Guided CoT Distillation) are well-defined and motivated.  MRPV, particularly, is a valuable contribution as it explicitly contrasts reasoning paths.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments span various reasoning tasks (mathematical, factual, general) and use both academic benchmarks and a new industrial dataset, lending credibility to the results.  The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component.
*   **Solid Analysis:** The authors delve into the performance of the model by categorizing errors based on their similarity with LLM predictions. This analysis helps to understand *why* the framework is effective.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Teacher LLMs:** While EAAI aims to reduce reliance on external guidance, the initial generation of CoTs still depends on an LLM teacher (GPT-4-turbo). How sensitive is ReaLM to the quality of this initial LLM and the temperature settings? The paper would benefit from a sensitivity analysis.
*   **Limited Theoretical Guarantees:** While the paper presents theoretical motivations (Propositions 2.1.1 and 2.1.2), these are somewhat high-level and rely on assumptions that may not always hold true in practice. A more rigorous theoretical analysis would strengthen the paper. The proof sketch provided in the appendix is also relatively basic.
*   **Proprietary Dataset:** The use of a proprietary industrial dataset, while relevant, limits reproducibility and the ability for others to directly compare their methods.
*   **Incremental Novelty:** The overall framework combines established techniques (RL, distillation, curriculum learning). The core novelty lies in *how* these are combined and adapted for SLMs, but the individual techniques are not groundbreaking.
*   **Generalizability of Vertical Domain Rules:** The paper targets vertical domains and distills expert knowledge into the model. It is not clear to what extent the presented framework is applicable to tasks that do not rely on any external knowledge or expert comment.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper offers a novel combination of techniques to improve SLM reasoning capabilities. The introduction of MRPV, the two-stage reward structure, and the EAAI training strategy, along with targeted CoT Distillation represents a worthwhile contribution. The empirical results are strong, showing consistent improvements across diverse tasks. The work is significant because it helps bridge the gap between SLMs and LLMs, enabling more cost-effective and accessible AI solutions. While not revolutionary, it's a well-engineered solution that pushes the boundaries of SLM capabilities. The paper could be more clear about the generalizability of its findings to contexts beyond vertical domains.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper addresses a practically significant problem with a well-designed framework and thorough experiments. While the individual techniques aren't entirely new, the integration of MRPV and EAAI is novel, and the results demonstrate tangible improvements. The reliance on teacher LLMs and the use of a proprietary dataset are limitations. The theoretical analysis is weak and the approach is somewhat incremental.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MedKGent: A Large Language Model Agent Framework for Constructing Temporally Evolving Medical Knowledge Graph
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12393v1)
- **Authors**: Duzhen Zhang, Zixiao Wang, Zhong-Zhi Li, Yahan Yu, Shuncheng Jia, Jiahua Dong, Haotian Xu, Xing Wu, Yingying Zhang, Tielin Zhang, Jie Yang, Xiuying Chen, Le Song
- **Abstract**: The rapid expansion of medical literature presents growing challenges for structuring and integrating domain knowledge at scale. Knowledge Graphs (KGs) offer a promising solution by enabling efficient retrieval, automated reasoning, and knowledge discovery. However, current KG construction methods often rely on supervised pipelines with limited generalizability or naively aggregate outputs from Large Language Models (LLMs), treating biomedical corpora as static and ignoring the temporal dynamics and contextual uncertainty of evolving knowledge. To address these limitations, we introduce MedKGent, a LLM agent framework for constructing temporally evolving medical KGs. Leveraging over 10 million PubMed abstracts published between 1975 and 2023, we simulate the emergence of biomedical knowledge via a fine-grained daily time series. MedKGent incrementally builds the KG in a day-by-day manner using two specialized agents powered by the Qwen2.5-32B-Instruct model. The Extractor Agent identifies knowledge triples and assigns confidence scores via sampling-based estimation, which are used to filter low-confidence extractions and inform downstream processing. The Constructor Agent incrementally integrates the retained triples into a temporally evolving graph, guided by confidence scores and timestamps to reinforce recurring knowledge and resolve conflicts. The resulting KG contains 156,275 entities and 2,971,384 relational triples. Quality assessments by two SOTA LLMs and three domain experts demonstrate an accuracy approaching 90\%, with strong inter-rater agreement. To evaluate downstream utility, we conduct RAG across seven medical question answering benchmarks using five leading LLMs, consistently observing significant improvements over non-augmented baselines. Case studies further demonstrate the KG's value in literature-based drug repurposing via confidence-aware causal inference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MedKGent, a novel framework that uses Large Language Model (LLM) agents to construct a temporally evolving medical knowledge graph (KG). Unlike traditional KG construction methods that often rely on static data or supervised pipelines, MedKGent leverages LLMs to incrementally build a KG from PubMed abstracts, capturing the dynamic evolution of biomedical knowledge over time.  The framework uses two main agents: an Extractor Agent to identify knowledge triples and assign confidence scores, and a Constructor Agent to integrate these triples into a temporal graph.  The resulting KG is significantly large, exceeding prior LLM-derived medical KGs. Rigorous quality assessments, including both LLM evaluations and expert reviews, demonstrate high accuracy and reliability. The paper further evaluates the downstream utility of MedKGent by integrating it into a Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) system for medical question answering. Results show consistent performance improvements across multiple benchmarks, indicating the practical value of the KG. A case study on drug repurposing illustrates how the temporal and semantic information within the KG can support novel hypothesis generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *temporal evolution* aspect of the KG construction process and using LLM agents to facilitate the process. Building medical KGs from text isn't new, and LLMs have been used for this purpose before. However, MedKGent distinguishes itself by explicitly modeling the temporal dynamics of medical knowledge. The sampling based confidence estimation is also a nice addition, providing a more nuanced evaluation compared to basic LLM outputs. The design of specialized agents is also a strength, enabling a more tailored approach to information extraction and integration.
* **Significance:** The potential significance is moderately high. The ability to track the evolution of medical knowledge is valuable for various applications. It can lead to more accurate and contextualized insights compared to KGs that treat medical literature as static. Also, its demonstration in a RAG setting strengthens the credibility of using MedKGent in real-world scenarios. The large scale of the KG makes it an attractive research resource.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Temporal Modeling:** Capturing the temporal evolution of knowledge is a significant advancement in medical KG construction.
    *   **LLM Agent Framework:** The use of specialized LLM agents allows for a modular and scalable approach.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The rigorous quality assessments, including both LLM evaluations and expert reviews, provide strong evidence of the KG's accuracy.
    *   **Downstream Utility:** Demonstrating consistent performance improvements in medical question answering highlights the practical value of the KG.
    *   **Case Study:** The drug repurposing case study effectively illustrates the potential of MedKGent for novel hypothesis generation.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on PubMed Abstracts:** The KG is limited by the information available in PubMed abstracts. Expanding the data sources to include full-text articles, clinical trial registries, or other relevant data could further enhance its comprehensiveness.
    *   **Dependency on PubTator3:** The study admits that extracting entities is done with the PubTator3 software. This process should be tested and vetted more.
    *   **Hallucination Risk:** The study uses language models which are prone to hallucinations, more specifically, generating false information, thus, decreasing the credibility of the information it is providing.

**Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of medical knowledge representation. By modeling the temporal evolution of medical knowledge, MedKGent offers a more nuanced and potentially more accurate representation than existing KGs. The rigorous evaluation and demonstration of downstream utility further strengthen the credibility of the framework. However, the weaknesses, particularly the reliance on PubMed abstracts and the potential for hallucinations, limit its overall impact. The innovation and improvements from pre-existing tools (like LLMs and PubTator3) are incremental, not revolutionary.
