- **Score**: 7/10

### Accelerating Edge Inference for Distributed MoE Models with Latency-Optimized Expert Placement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12851v1)
- **Authors**: Tian Wu, Liming Wang, Zijian Wen, Xiaoxi Zhang, Jingpu Duan, Xianwei Zhang, Jinhang Zuo
- **Abstract**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) have become a cornerstone for training and scaling large language models (LLMs), offering substantial gains in model capacity and efficiency through sparse expert activation. However, serving these models remains challenging in practice, particularly in resource-constrained edge environments, due to their large memory footprint and complex communication demands. While centralized cloud inference is common, it incurs high infrastructure costs, along with latency and privacy concerns. A few recent edge MoE works propose memory-efficient strategies but typically focus on single-device or homogeneous setups. This paper presents DanceMoE, an efficient MoE inference framework that enables activation-aware expert placement across collaborative, heterogeneous, GPU-equipped edge servers. DanceMoE leverages the inherent sparsity of MoE models and workload locality to minimize cross-server communication and enable efficient expert placement under heterogeneous resource constraints. It introduces a data-driven, activation-aware placement algorithm that balances local coverage and memory usage across servers, alongside a lightweight migration mechanism that adapts expert assignments under evolving workloads. We evaluate DanceMoE on modern MoE models and widely used datasets, demonstrating up to 30.6\% lower inference latency, and substantial communication reduction compared to state-of-the-art baselines, showcasing the effectiveness of collaborative edge-based MoE inference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DanceMoE, a novel framework for efficient Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) inference in resource-constrained, collaborative edge environments.  DanceMoE addresses the challenges of large MoE model sizes, high communication overhead, and heterogeneous resource availability in edge deployments. It achieves this by: (1) enabling activation-aware expert placement across collaborative edge servers, (2) minimizing cross-server communication through exploiting MoE sparsity and workload locality, (3) employing a data-driven placement algorithm that balances local coverage and memory usage, and (4) implementing a lightweight migration mechanism to adapt expert assignments to evolving workloads.  The paper presents experimental results demonstrating lower inference latency and substantial communication reduction compared to state-of-the-art baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the combination of several techniques tailored specifically to collaborative edge MoE inference.  While individual components like expert placement and migration have been explored in other contexts (datacenter, single device), their integration and adaptation to the unique constraints of edge computing (heterogeneity, limited bandwidth, resource constraints) is a significant contribution. DanceMoE's activation-aware approach and integration with lightweight migration algorithms are especially novel. The integration of an activation-aware algorithm tailored to heterogeneous devices makes it more suitable for the real-world dynamics in edge environments.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a very relevant and important problem.  MoE models are becoming increasingly important, but their size makes edge deployment difficult.  By enabling efficient edge inference, DanceMoE has the potential to reduce reliance on cloud infrastructure, lower latency, improve data privacy, and decrease costs. The improvements shown through the experimental results, while perhaps not revolutionary in magnitude (some results show marginal gains), are still practically significant and highlight the effectiveness of their solution.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Practical Problem:**  The paper tackles a practical and increasingly important problem in the field of large language model deployment.
    *   **Comprehensive Solution:** DanceMoE provides a complete system, addressing placement, communication, and adaptation.
    *   **Data-Driven Approach:** The activation-aware placement strategy leverages real-world workload patterns for improved performance.
    *   **Theoretical Guarantees:**  The paper provides theoretical approximations and guarantees regarding the placement algorithm.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation:** The experiments are reasonably comprehensive, using modern MoE models, diverse datasets, and comparisons against relevant baselines.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Simulation vs. Real-World:** While a testbed is used, the reliance on simulations (especially for scalability analysis) is a potential weakness.  Simulated environments may not fully capture the complexities of real-world edge deployments (network congestion, hardware variability, background processes).
    *   **Bandwidth Limitation:**  The bandwidth limitation in the testbed might not realistically emulate edge network conditions across diverse environments. Although a range of settings were used it is still not perfectly representative.
    *   **Incremental Gains:** While the overall approach is novel, the performance gains shown in some experiments are modest, especially with the Mixtral model, suggesting room for further optimization.
    *   **Limited Migration Policy:** The migration policy seems relatively simple. More sophisticated migration strategies that consider the "cost" of migration in more detail might improve performance further.
    *   **Scalability assumptions:** The scalability of the model to larger numbers of heterogeneous edge servers is not completely substantiated. The experiments show the efficacy with respect to individual components of the framework but additional scalability experiments would strengthen the claims.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future edge-based MoE inference systems. The concepts of activation-aware placement, lightweight migration, and collaborative inference are valuable and likely to be adopted in other research and practical implementations. The work also highlights the importance of considering system heterogeneity and workload dynamics in edge environments.

**Justification for Score:**

DanceMoE presents a significant contribution to the field of edge-based MoE inference. The integration of multiple techniques tailored to the specific challenges of edge computing demonstrates novelty and practicality. While the performance gains in some experiments are not always overwhelming, the comprehensive approach and theoretical foundation contribute to the paper's overall value. The identified weaknesses, while present, do not detract significantly from the overall contribution. Therefore, a score of 7 reflects the importance and potential impact of the work, while acknowledging the room for future refinement and more extensive real-world validation.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 7/10

### E3RG: Building Explicit Emotion-driven Empathetic Response Generation System with Multimodal Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12854v1)
- **Authors**: Ronghao Lin, Shuai Shen, Weipeng Hu, Qiaolin He, Aolin Xiong, Li Huang, Haifeng Hu, Yap-peng Tan
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Empathetic Response Generation (MERG) is crucial for building emotionally intelligent human-computer interactions. Although large language models (LLMs) have improved text-based ERG, challenges remain in handling multimodal emotional content and maintaining identity consistency. Thus, we propose E3RG, an Explicit Emotion-driven Empathetic Response Generation System based on multimodal LLMs which decomposes MERG task into three parts: multimodal empathy understanding, empathy memory retrieval, and multimodal response generation. By integrating advanced expressive speech and video generative models, E3RG delivers natural, emotionally rich, and identity-consistent responses without extra training. Experiments validate the superiority of our system on both zero-shot and few-shot settings, securing Top-1 position in the Avatar-based Multimodal Empathy Challenge on ACM MM 25. Our code is available at https://github.com/RH-Lin/E3RG.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces E3RG, an Explicit Emotion-driven Empathetic Response Generation system built on multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  E3RG addresses the challenges of generating empathetic and consistent multimodal responses (text, speech, and video) in human-computer interaction. The system decomposes the MERG task into three sub-tasks: Multimodal Empathy Understanding (MEU), Empathy Memory Retrieval (EMR), and Multimodal Empathy Generation (MEG).  MEU utilizes MLLMs to understand context and predict user emotions. EMR maintains consistency of dialogue, identity and  a cache of prior generated responses.  MEG combines emotion predictions with generative models for expressive speech (OpenVoice) and emotionally aligned talking-head videos (DICE-Talk).  The system is evaluated in zero-shot and few-shot settings, achieving competitive results and Top-1 position in the Avatar-based Multimodal Empathy Challenge on ACM MM’25.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a well-structured system, E3RG, by breaking the MERG task into sub-tasks, which is a reasonable engineering approach. The novelty lies in the explicit separation and combination of established components (MLLMs, speech synthesis, talking-head generation) with specific guidance (emotion prediction and memory retrieval) to enhance empathy. While the individual components aren't groundbreaking, the specific architecture and the explicit emotion-driven approach is fairly novel.
*   **Significance:**  The significance lies in addressing the consistency and emotional richness issues in MERG.  The modular approach allows for easier improvement and replacement of individual components. The system's practical success in a competitive setting (ACM MM'25 challenge) suggests its value in real-world applications. The modularity also could influence future research by providing a framework for controlled experimentation on individual aspects of MERG. However, it would have been useful to have ablations of each component and stage separately.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Modular Design:** The three-stage breakdown (MEU, EMR, MEG) provides a clear and adaptable architecture.
    *   **Explicit Emotion Guidance:**  Incorporating emotion prediction and pre-defined emotion banks guides generation models.
    *   **Consistency Maintenance:** The memory retrieval component addresses the challenge of maintaining identity and dialogue consistency.
    *   **Practical Success:** The top-1 position in a competitive challenge validates the system's efficacy.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Existing Models:** The system leverages existing MLLMs, speech synthesis and talking-head generation models, which limits innovation on the foundational building blocks.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** The paper lacks detailed ablation studies to quantify the contribution of each component.
    *   **Lack of theoretical analysis:** Although impressive, it would be useful to have theoretical insights and analyses into the system.

*   **Impact:** The paper could inspire further research in explicitly controlled multimodal generation and empathetic human-computer interaction. The success in the ACM MM'25 challenge may encourage the adoption of the modular approach in MERG.

*   **Other Comments:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the system architecture and evaluation results. The focus on training-free use of MLLMs is a bonus, but this setting does not lend itself to much innovation on MLLMs itself.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The E3RG system represents a solid engineering achievement in the field of multimodal empathetic response generation. While it does not introduce groundbreaking theoretical insights or fundamentally new models (it relies on existing components), it offers a valuable architectural contribution through its modular design and explicit emotion guidance. The system’s practical success in a competitive benchmark supports its effectiveness. The paper's impact is primarily on the applied side, offering a promising framework for building more natural and emotionally aware conversational agents, but is somewhat incremental. The lack of theoretical analyses and detail about each sub-component limits the potential broader impact.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### S^2-Guidance: Stochastic Self Guidance for Training-Free Enhancement of Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12880v1)
- **Authors**: Chubin Chen, Jiashu Zhu, Xiaokun Feng, Nisha Huang, Meiqi Wu, Fangyuan Mao, Jiahong Wu, Xiangxiang Chu, Xiu Li
- **Abstract**: Classifier-free Guidance (CFG) is a widely used technique in modern diffusion models for enhancing sample quality and prompt adherence. However, through an empirical analysis on Gaussian mixture modeling with a closed-form solution, we observe a discrepancy between the suboptimal results produced by CFG and the ground truth. The model's excessive reliance on these suboptimal predictions often leads to semantic incoherence and low-quality outputs. To address this issue, we first empirically demonstrate that the model's suboptimal predictions can be effectively refined using sub-networks of the model itself. Building on this insight, we propose S^2-Guidance, a novel method that leverages stochastic block-dropping during the forward process to construct stochastic sub-networks, effectively guiding the model away from potential low-quality predictions and toward high-quality outputs. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments on text-to-image and text-to-video generation tasks demonstrate that S^2-Guidance delivers superior performance, consistently surpassing CFG and other advanced guidance strategies. Our code will be released.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "S2-Guidance: Stochastic Self Guidance for Training-Free Enhancement of Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces S2-Guidance, a novel training-free technique to enhance the quality and prompt adherence of diffusion models.  It addresses the limitations of Classifier-Free Guidance (CFG), which can lead to semantic incoherence and a loss of fine details.  S2-Guidance leverages stochastic block-dropping during the forward process to construct sub-networks within the diffusion model itself. These sub-networks act as "weak models" that guide the main model away from potentially suboptimal predictions, leading to higher quality outputs. The method is evaluated on text-to-image and text-to-video generation tasks, demonstrating superior performance compared to CFG and other guidance strategies. A key benefit is that S2-Guidance is training-free and can be easily adapted to various generative models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a novel approach to improving diffusion model guidance. The core idea of using stochastic block-dropping to create self-corrective sub-networks is intriguing and distinguishes it from existing guidance methods like Autoguidance, which rely on externally trained or manually tuned weak models. The connection to Bayesian approximation theory and the derivation of the method as an uncertainty-aware correction is also a significant contribution, giving the technique a strong theoretical grounding.

**Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in the potential for widespread adoption due to its training-free nature and ease of integration. Improving sample quality and prompt adherence without requiring retraining is a valuable contribution. The demonstrated improvements on both text-to-image and text-to-video generation are also significant, showcasing the generalizability of the approach. The thorough empirical evaluation, including quantitative metrics, qualitative comparisons, and user studies, further strengthens the claims.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using stochastic block dropping to create self-correcting subnetworks within the model is a novel contribution.
*   **Theoretical grounding:** The paper provides a solid theoretical justification for the method based on Bayesian approximation theory.
*   **Training-free:** S2-Guidance offers a training-free enhancement, making it easily adaptable to pre-trained diffusion models.
*   **Generalizability:** The method is shown to be effective on both text-to-image and text-to-video tasks.
*   **Extensive Evaluation:** A comprehensive set of experiments, including quantitative metrics, qualitative comparisons, and user studies, supports the claims.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational overhead (mitigated):** The initial "Naive S2-Guidance" approach has significant computational overhead, which is partially addressed by the simplified "S2-Guidance." It could be even more beneficial to fully tackle the speed limitations.
*   **Parameter tuning (addressed, but potentially delicate):**  The S2 Scale (w) parameter requires some tuning, although the ablation study shows it is relatively robust. Further investigation into adaptive or automated tuning strategies could be valuable.
*   **Limited insights into block importance:** While the method works, the paper does not provide deep insights into what blocks are more important for improvement, if there is a specific architecture that is better suited, or specific prompts to target.

**Potential Influence:** This work could significantly influence the field of diffusion model research, as it provides a practical and theoretically sound method to improve existing models without the need for retraining.  It is likely to inspire further research into self-guidance techniques and methods for leveraging the internal structure of diffusion models for improved performance.  Additionally, It would benefit the community to have more insights in to what blocks/architecture pairs lead to the best performance.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

S2-Guidance demonstrates significant novelty and practical value. The theoretically grounded approach and training-free nature are highly desirable. While the initial computational overhead was a weakness, it was addressed by S2-Guidance. There are clear opportunities for further research, such as exploring adaptive parameter tuning and further optimizing the block dropping strategy. The current iteration deserves a score of 8, reflecting its significant contribution to the field, balanced with the areas that could be improved and explored in future research.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### A Stitch in Time Saves Nine: Proactive Self-Refinement for Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12903v1)
- **Authors**: Jinyi Han, Xinyi Wang, Haiquan Zhao, Tingyun li, Zishang Jiang, Sihang Jiang, Jiaqing Liang, Xin Lin, Weikang Zhou, Zeye Sun, Fei Yu, Yanghua Xiao
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in self-refinement have demonstrated significant potential for improving the outputs of large language models (LLMs) through iterative refinement. However, most existing self-refinement methods rely on a reactive process with a fixed number of iterations, making it difficult to determine the optimal timing and content of refinement based on the evolving generation context. Inspired by the way humans dynamically refine their thoughts during execution, we propose ProActive Self-Refinement (PASR), a novel method that enables LLMs to refine their outputs during the generation process. Unlike methods that regenerate entire responses, PASR proactively decides whether, when, and how to refine based on the model's internal state and evolving context. We conduct extensive experiments on a diverse set of 10 tasks to evaluate the effectiveness of PASR. Experimental results show that PASR significantly enhances problem-solving performance. In particular, on Qwen3-8B, PASR reduces average token consumption by 41.6 percent compared to standard generation, while also achieving an 8.2 percent improvement in accuracy. Our code and all baselines used in the paper are available in the GitHub.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary of the paper followed by a rigorous and critical evaluation of its novelty and significance:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ProActive Self-Refinement (PASR), a novel reinforcement learning (RL) method designed to enable large language models (LLMs) to proactively refine their outputs during the generation process. Unlike traditional post-hoc self-refinement techniques that iteratively improve a completed response, PASR empowers LLMs to autonomously decide *whether*, *when*, and *how* to refine their reasoning based on the model's internal state and evolving context. This is achieved through a structured output format with `<think>`, `<refine>`, and `<answer>` tags and a comparison-based reward strategy. The method is evaluated on a diverse set of 10 tasks and demonstrates improvements in problem-solving performance, reduced token consumption, and enhanced accuracy compared to standard generation and existing self-refinement methods. The paper emphasizes PASR's ability to dynamically detect and correct reasoning errors and its domain-agnostic generalization capabilities.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a critical limitation in existing LLM self-refinement approaches – the reactive and often inflexible nature of post-hoc refinement. By proposing a method that allows for proactive and context-aware refinement during the generation process, the paper offers a valuable advancement.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of *proactive* self-refinement is the main strength of the paper. PASR departs from the standard post-hoc paradigm, enabling dynamic decision-making regarding refinement.
*   **Methodological Soundness:** The RL-based training approach, combined with a structured output format and comparison-based reward strategy, provides a well-defined and theoretically motivated framework.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The extensive experimental results on a diverse set of tasks provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of PASR, demonstrating improvements in accuracy, reduced token usage, and domain-agnostic generalization. The paper's ability to retain or even improve performance while reducing token usage is especially important. This demonstrates greater efficiency and reduced computational costs.
*   **Reproducibility:** The authors make their code and baselines publicly available, enhancing the reproducibility and accessibility of the research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:** The RL framework introduces additional complexity compared to simpler self-refinement techniques. The reward design is also sophisticated and could be sensitive to hyperparameter tuning. It adds complexity but also is the source of the improvements.
*   **Limited Analysis of Failure Cases:** While the paper mentions failure cases related to knowledge limitations and metacognitive abilities, a more in-depth analysis of the types of errors that PASR *cannot* correct and the factors contributing to these failures would further strengthen the work.
*   **Judge Model Reliance:** The accuracy reward is heavily dependent on the quality of a judge model and its ability to accurately assess generated answers. While the paper validates the judge model's reliability through human annotation, the dependence on another large model can introduce biases and limit the scalability of the approach.
*   **Incremental improvement compared to other approaches**. In some instances, the performance improvement is not significant compared to prior work.

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to the growing field of LLM self-improvement and offers a promising direction for developing more autonomous and robust AI agents. PASR's ability to dynamically refine reasoning processes has the potential to enhance the performance of LLMs in various real-world applications and opens up new avenues for research in areas such as explainable AI, active learning, and human-AI collaboration. The paper highlights the importance of developing fine-grained mechanisms to encourage meaningful and constructive refinement while discouraging both excessive and insufficient refinement.

**Justification for the Score:**

Given the novelty of the proactive self-refinement concept, the methodological soundness of the RL-based training approach, the comprehensive empirical validation, and the potential for impact on the field of LLM self-improvement, the paper merits a **Score: 8**. While there are some limitations in complexity, reliance on a judge model, and incremental improvement, the overall contribution is significant and represents a valuable advancement in the field. The detailed analysis and solid experimental design contribute meaningfully to the understanding and development of LLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### SecFSM: Knowledge Graph-Guided Verilog Code Generation for Secure Finite State Machines in Systems-on-Chip
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12910v1)
- **Authors**: Ziteng Hu, Yingjie Xia, Xiyuan Chen, Li Kuang
- **Abstract**: Finite State Machines (FSMs) play a critical role in implementing control logic for Systems-on-Chip (SoC). Traditionally, FSMs are implemented by hardware engineers through Verilog coding, which is often tedious and time-consuming. Recently, with the remarkable progress of Large Language Models (LLMs) in code generation, LLMs have been increasingly explored for automating Verilog code generation. However, LLM-generated Verilog code often suffers from security vulnerabilities, which is particularly concerning for security-sensitive FSM implementations. To address this issue, we propose SecFSM, a novel method that leverages a security-oriented knowledge graph to guide LLMs in generating more secure Verilog code. Specifically, we first construct a FSM Security Knowledge Graph (FSKG) as an external aid to LLMs. Subsequently, we analyze users' requirements to identify vulnerabilities and get a list of vulnerabilities in the requirements. Then, we retrieve knowledge from FSKG based on the vulnerabilities list. Finally, we construct security prompts based on the security knowledge for Verilog code generation. To evaluate SecFSM, we build a dedicated dataset collected from academic datasets, artificial datasets, papers, and industrial cases. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SecFSM outperforms state-of-the-art baselines. In particular, on a benchmark of 25 security test cases evaluated by DeepSeek-R1, SecFSM achieves an outstanding pass rate of 21/25.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SecFSM: Knowledge Graph-Guided Verilog Code Generation for Secure Finite State Machines in Systems-on-Chip" introduces a novel method, SecFSM, to generate more secure Verilog code for finite state machines (FSMs) using large language models (LLMs). SecFSM leverages a security-oriented knowledge graph (FSKG) as an external aid. The method involves pre-analysis to identify potential vulnerabilities in user requirements, knowledge retrieval from FSKG based on these vulnerabilities, and the construction of security prompts to guide LLMs during code generation.  The authors created a dedicated dataset and demonstrated that SecFSM outperforms baseline methods in generating secure Verilog code, particularly on security test cases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the integration of a security-focused knowledge graph with vulnerability pre-analysis to guide LLMs in generating more secure Verilog code for FSMs. While using knowledge graphs and LLMs for code generation isn't entirely new, the specific focus on *security* vulnerabilities in FSM *Verilog* implementations is a distinct and worthwhile contribution. The approach of vulnerability *pre-analysis* to tailor the knowledge retrieval is also a novel and important aspect.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the increasing reliance on FSMs in SoCs and the potential security risks associated with vulnerabilities in these FSM implementations. LLMs are becoming increasingly popular for automating Verilog generation; thus, a method that enhances the security of LLM-generated code has considerable practical value. The experimental results, showing improved security performance compared to baselines, support the practical significance of SecFSM.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of security vulnerabilities in LLM-generated Verilog code for FSMs.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** SecFSM's methodology is well-structured and clearly explained, including pre-analysis, knowledge retrieval, and prompt engineering.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper uses a custom dataset comprising academic, artificial, and industrial cases, offering a robust evaluation of the proposed method. The use of established metrics (Functionality and Security) aids in comparing results.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contribution of each module within SecFSM. The experiments show that pre-analysis and retrieval module both contribute significantly to SecFSM's performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Knowledge Graph Construction Detail:** The construction of the FSKG, while mentioned as involving manual screening, could benefit from more detail about the types of security knowledge included, the challenges in knowledge representation, and the entity alignment process. How was the information in the FSKG validated? This information is critical to assessing the scalability of the approach and the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Limited Generalization Concerns:** The experimental evaluation should explicitly analyze the limitations of the approach. Specifically, in the case study, the method struggles when requirements are described with nonstandard formats. A more thorough discussion about why this occurs would greatly benefit the community. What specific types of coding vulnerabilities are best handled by SecFSM?
    *  **Code Generation Metrics:** More discussion related to the efficiency (runtime, memory footprint, etc.) of generated Verilog code would have strengthened the manuscript.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research in secure hardware design automation and LLM-based code generation.  It provides a concrete example of how domain knowledge and problem-specific analysis can be integrated to improve the security of LLM-generated hardware.  The FSKG concept could be extended to other hardware security domains. The code, upon its release, will likely be utilized as a reference by other researchers.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the intersection of hardware security and LLM-based code generation. The approach is novel, the methodology is sound, and the evaluation is reasonably comprehensive. While there are areas for improvement (knowledge graph details, discussion about the limitations of the analysis module, inclusion of code efficiency metrics), the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The potential impact on the field warrants a positive evaluation.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### FoleySpace: Vision-Aligned Binaural Spatial Audio Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12918v1)
- **Authors**: Lei Zhao, Rujin Chen, Chi Zhang, Xiao-Lei Zhang, Xuelong Li
- **Abstract**: Recently, with the advancement of AIGC, deep learning-based video-to-audio (V2A) technology has garnered significant attention. However, existing research mostly focuses on mono audio generation that lacks spatial perception, while the exploration of binaural spatial audio generation technologies, which can provide a stronger sense of immersion, remains insufficient. To solve this problem, we propose FoleySpace, a framework for video-to-binaural audio generation that produces immersive and spatially consistent stereo sound guided by visual information. Specifically, we develop a sound source estimation method to determine the sound source 2D coordinates and depth in each video frame, and then employ a coordinate mapping mechanism to convert the 2D source positions into a 3D trajectory. This 3D trajectory, together with the monaural audio generated by a pre-trained V2A model, serves as a conditioning input for a diffusion model to generate spatially consistent binaural audio. To support the generation of dynamic sound fields, we constructed a training dataset based on recorded Head-Related Impulse Responses that includes various sound source movement scenarios. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms existing approaches in spatial perception consistency, effectively enhancing the immersive quality of the audio-visual experience.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the FoleySpace paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FoleySpace, a novel framework for generating binaural spatial audio from video input. This framework addresses the limitations of existing video-to-audio (V2A) techniques, which primarily focus on generating monaural audio and lack spatial awareness. FoleySpace estimates the 3D trajectory of sound sources in the video by combining object detection (YOLO-World) and monocular depth estimation (DepthMaster). This trajectory, along with the monaural audio generated by a pre-trained V2A model (MMAudio), is used as a conditioning input for a diffusion model (DiffWave) to generate spatially consistent binaural audio. The paper also describes the creation of a new binaural audio training dataset based on Head-Related Impulse Responses (HRIRs) that incorporates dynamic sound source movements. Experiments demonstrate that FoleySpace outperforms existing methods in spatial perception consistency and enhances the immersive quality of the audio-visual experience. The paper also explores a coarse-grained implementation of the proposed method, striking a better balance between precision and computational efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the integrated approach of using visual information to drive the generation of binaural spatial audio in a V2A setting. The key innovations are:

    *   A pipeline combining object detection and depth estimation to extract sound source trajectories directly from video, bypassing the need for external spatial parameters or camera metadata.
    *   A mechanism to map 2D video coordinates to a 3D sound field.
    *   Utilizing the estimated 3D trajectory alongside monaural audio as a condition for a diffusion model to generate binaural audio with spatial awareness.
    *   The creation of a suitable HRIR-based dataset specifically designed for training dynamic sound field generation.

    While the individual components (YOLO, DepthMaster, DiffWave) are not novel, their combination and application to this specific problem of binaural audio generation from video *is* innovative. Specifically, creating the dynamic sound-field HRIR dataset to train dynamic spatial audio generation enhances the paper's novelty. The exploration of a coarse-grained implementation further strengthens the practical contributions of the paper.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of V2A research by addressing the critical gap of spatial audio generation. This has substantial implications for:

    *   **Immersive XR applications:** Enables the creation of more realistic and engaging virtual environments.
    *   **Interactive entertainment:** Enhances the user experience in games and other interactive media.
    *   **Dynamic media production:** Automates the creation of spatially accurate audio for video content.

    The experimental results support the claim that FoleySpace enhances spatial perception and immersion, as evidenced by user study metrics and objective evaluations.  The comparison with existing methods clearly demonstrates the advantages of the proposed approach, which effectively preserves audio quality while significantly improving spatial consistency.

    *   The user studies, although using a reasonable number of participants (24), are still relatively small and could be expanded for increased statistical power.  The objective metrics are relevant, but the reliance on metrics that are sensitive to feature extractors (FD variants) introduces a potential bias. While KL divergence is used, alternative diversity metrics would strengthen the quantitative analysis.

    *   The paper demonstrates a promising solution to a significant problem and provides a clear path for future research in spatial audio generation. However, the integration of additional cues to improve the realism and accuracy of the generated audio (e.g., scene understanding, room acoustics, material properties) is left as future work.

**Justification for Score:**

FoleySpace presents a solid contribution to the field. The integrated approach of visual information-driven binaural audio generation is well-motivated, technically sound, and experimentally validated. The paper is relatively easy to read and understand, although it has some issues with clarity in its presentation. Furthermore, a more in-depth study on the impact of individual framework components would strengthen the findings. The work is still primarily incremental and builds upon existing techniques. Therefore, the contributions do not meet the criteria of an 'exceptional' paper.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### 7Bench: a Comprehensive Benchmark for Layout-guided Text-to-image Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12919v1)
- **Authors**: Elena Izzo, Luca Parolari, Davide Vezzaro, Lamberto Ballan
- **Abstract**: Layout-guided text-to-image models offer greater control over the generation process by explicitly conditioning image synthesis on the spatial arrangement of elements. As a result, their adoption has increased in many computer vision applications, ranging from content creation to synthetic data generation. A critical challenge is achieving precise alignment between the image, textual prompt, and layout, ensuring semantic fidelity and spatial accuracy. Although recent benchmarks assess text alignment, layout alignment remains overlooked, and no existing benchmark jointly evaluates both. This gap limits the ability to evaluate a model's spatial fidelity, which is crucial when using layout-guided generation for synthetic data, as errors can introduce noise and degrade data quality. In this work, we introduce 7Bench, the first benchmark to assess both semantic and spatial alignment in layout-guided text-to-image generation. It features text-and-layout pairs spanning seven challenging scenarios, investigating object generation, color fidelity, attribute recognition, inter-object relationships, and spatial control. We propose an evaluation protocol that builds on existing frameworks by incorporating the layout alignment score to assess spatial accuracy. Using 7Bench, we evaluate several state-of-the-art diffusion models, uncovering their respective strengths and limitations across diverse alignment tasks. The benchmark is available at https://github.com/Elizzo/7Bench.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces 7Bench, a new benchmark designed to comprehensively evaluate layout-guided text-to-image models. It addresses the current lack of benchmarks that jointly assess both semantic (text) and spatial (layout) alignment in generated images. 7Bench features text-and-layout pairs across seven challenging scenarios: object generation, color fidelity, attribute recognition, inter-object relationships, and spatial control.  The authors propose an evaluation protocol that builds on existing frameworks by incorporating a layout alignment score alongside a text alignment score. The paper evaluates several state-of-the-art diffusion models using 7Bench, revealing their strengths and limitations in different alignment tasks. The benchmark dataset and evaluation code are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the creation of a dedicated benchmark specifically designed for layout-guided text-to-image models. While previous works have evaluated text-to-image generation and some have explored aspects of layout control, this is the first comprehensive attempt to evaluate both aspects together. The defined scenarios appear reasonable and encompass relevant aspects of layout fidelity.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of this work is that it fills a crucial gap in the evaluation of layout-guided image generation. Current research often relies on ad-hoc or incomplete evaluation methods. By providing a standardized benchmark, this work enables more rigorous comparisons between different models and facilitates progress in the field. The focus on both text and layout alignment is particularly important for applications such as synthetic data generation where precise spatial arrangements are critical.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:**  7Bench considers multiple challenging scenarios relevant to layout-guided generation.
    *   **Clear Evaluation Protocol:**  The paper defines a clear and reproducible evaluation protocol based on established metrics (TIFA and object detection with IoU). The combined text and layout alignment scores provide a more holistic view of model performance.
    *   **Public Availability:** The public release of the benchmark dataset and code is a significant strength, encouraging adoption and further development.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:**  The paper presents experimental results on several state-of-the-art models, highlighting their strengths and weaknesses and demonstrating the utility of the benchmark.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on External Metrics:** The evaluation protocol relies on existing metrics like TIFA and object detection. While this builds upon established methods, the performance of the benchmark is dependent on the accuracy and robustness of these underlying tools. If the underlying methods exhibit bias, or are not robust across all cases, that will influence the evaluation.
    *   **Potential for Bias in Prompts and Layouts:** The dataset's prompt and layout creation process, while described, could be subject to biases. A larger, more diverse, and potentially automatically generated dataset could further strengthen the benchmark.
    *   **Limited Model Analysis:** Although several models are tested, deeper analysis of model failure modes and the impact of different architectural choices on layout fidelity could be more impactful.
    *  **Dataset Scale:** While the benchmark is comprehensive, its scale might be seen as a limitation. 224 prompts might not cover all possible layout and object scenarios.

*   **Potential Influence:**  7Bench has the potential to become a standard benchmark for layout-guided text-to-image models. This could significantly improve the rigor and comparability of research in this area, leading to faster progress in the development of more controllable and reliable image generation techniques. The benchmark could inspire further extensions and refinements, such as incorporating more complex layout representations or focusing on specific downstream applications.

**Justification:**

The paper offers a significant contribution to the community by addressing a clear need for a comprehensive evaluation framework for layout-guided text-to-image models. While it builds upon existing metrics and the dataset size isn't vast, the meticulous design of scenarios and the joint consideration of text and layout alignment represent a notable advance. The public release of the benchmark will likely have a positive impact on future research, making the evaluation much more rigorous. The benchmark addresses a critical gap in the landscape and has the potential to influence the course of research for years to come.

### RUM: Rule+LLM-Based Comprehensive Assessment on Testing Skills
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12922v1)
- **Authors**: Yue Wang, Zhenyu Chen, Yuan Zhao, Chunrong Fang, Ziyuan Wang, Song Huang
- **Abstract**: Over the past eight years, the META method has served as a multidimensional testing skill assessment system in the National College Student Contest on Software Testing, successfully assessing over 100,000 students' testing skills. However, META is primarily limited to the objective assessment of test scripts, lacking the ability to automatically assess subjective aspects such as test case and test report. To address this limitation, this paper proposes RUM, a comprehensive assessment approach that combines rules and large language models (LLMs). RUM achieves a comprehensive assessment by rapidly processing objective indicators through rules while utilizing LLMs for in-depth subjective analysis of test case documents, test scripts, and test reports. The experimental results show that compared to traditional manual testing skill assessment, RUM improves assessment efficiency by 80.77\% and reduces costs by 97.38\%, while maintaining high accuracy and consistency of assessment. By applying RUM on the contest on software testing, we find that it not only enhances the efficiency and scalability of skill assessment in software testing education, but also provides teachers with more comprehensive and objective evidence for student ability assessment, facilitating personalized teaching and learning. This study offers new insights into the assessment of testing skills, which are expected to promote further development in test process optimization and software quality assurance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "RUM: Rule+LLM-Based Comprehensive Assessment on Testing Skills":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RUM, a new approach for comprehensively assessing software testing skills by combining rule-based methods and large language models (LLMs).  It addresses the limitations of existing systems like META, which primarily focus on objective assessment of test scripts and lack the ability to automatically assess subjective aspects such as test case design and test report quality. RUM leverages rules to efficiently process objective indicators while employing LLMs for in-depth subjective analysis.  Experimental results demonstrate that RUM improves assessment efficiency and reduces costs compared to manual assessment, while maintaining high accuracy and consistency.  The paper also describes a real-world application of RUM in a national software testing contest.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the integration of rule-based and LLM-based assessment for software testing skills. While both types of assessments exist, their combination in this specific domain is a worthwhile contribution. The idea of using rules for efficiency and LLMs for subjective aspects is logical and well-motivated. The concept of prompt engineering for LLMs to generate rule-based assessment code is also fairly innovative. The pre-processing steps to unify test case documents is not particularly novel, as document parsers have been around for some time.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to be significant for software testing education and potentially for industrial training. The ability to automate the assessment of subjective testing skills is a valuable contribution, particularly given the increasing scale of testing education programs. The demonstrated efficiency and cost reductions are strong selling points. However, the long-term impact will depend on how easily RUM can be adapted to different testing contexts and languages. The focus of the case study being a Chinese contest could limit adoption to wider contexts without sufficient evaluation of cross-cultural generalizability. Also, the results need to be reproducible, and the prompt templates needs to be shared or more clearly described for the result to be valid.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly identifies a gap in existing testing skill assessment systems.
    *   The RUM approach is well-motivated and explained.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate improved efficiency and cost-effectiveness.
    *   The real-world application in a national contest adds credibility.
    *   The paper comprehensively evaluates the approach against various metrics like accuracy, consistency, and stability.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper lacks information and justification for the LLM model used (moonshot-v1-8k). Is it a public model? The paper should discuss more detail the prompt template. The reproducibility and evaluation of performance of the LLM needs to be clear.
    *   While the experimental results are promising, more detailed information about the dataset and the specific testing skills being assessed would be beneficial. Is the dataset publicly available? If not, are more detail provided about the dataset, which are the main assessment skills that have been assessed?
    *   The generalizability of RUM to different testing domains and programming languages needs to be explored further. Also, the system have only been evaluate using Chinese language. If so, more discussion and justifcation are needed in the paper.
    *   A more in-depth discussion of the limitations of LLMs and how RUM mitigates those limitations (e.g., bias, hallucinations) would strengthen the paper.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of software testing education, but the novelty is a combination of known techniques, and the impact will depend on generalizability and reproducibility. The paper should justify more detail on the system, the LLM model used, and experiment details.

### SEDEG:Sequential Enhancement of Decoder and Encoder's Generality for Class Incremental Learning with Small Memory
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12932v1)
- **Authors**: Hongyang Chen, Shaoling Pu, Lingyu Zheng, Zhongwu Sun
- **Abstract**: In incremental learning, enhancing the generality of knowledge is crucial for adapting to dynamic data inputs. It can develop generalized representations or more balanced decision boundaries, preventing the degradation of long-term knowledge over time and thus mitigating catastrophic forgetting. Some emerging incremental learning methods adopt an encoder-decoder architecture and have achieved promising results. In the encoder-decoder achitecture, improving the generalization capabilities of both the encoder and decoder is critical, as it helps preserve previously learned knowledge while ensuring adaptability and robustness to new, diverse data inputs. However, many existing continual methods focus solely on enhancing one of the two components, which limits their effectiveness in mitigating catastrophic forgetting. And these methods perform even worse in small-memory scenarios, where only a limited number of historical samples can be stored. To mitigate this limitation, we introduces SEDEG, a two-stage training framework for vision transformers (ViT), focusing on sequentially improving the generality of both Decoder and Encoder. Initially, SEDEG trains an ensembled encoder through feature boosting to learn generalized representations, which subsequently enhance the decoder's generality and balance the classifier. The next stage involves using knowledge distillation (KD) strategies to compress the ensembled encoder and develop a new, more generalized encoder. This involves using a balanced KD approach and feature KD for effective knowledge transfer. Extensive experiments on three benchmark datasets show SEDEG's superior performance, and ablation studies confirm the efficacy of its components. The code is available at https://github.com/ShaolingPu/CIL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SEDEG: Sequential Enhancement of Decoder and Encoder's Generality for Class Incremental Learning with Small Memory" addresses the problem of catastrophic forgetting in class-incremental learning, particularly in scenarios with limited memory.  It proposes a two-stage training framework called SEDEG, designed for vision transformers (ViTs). The first stage focuses on training an ensembled encoder using feature boosting to learn generalized representations. This, in turn, enhances the generality of the decoder and balances the classifier. The second stage employs knowledge distillation (KD) strategies, specifically a balanced KD approach and feature KD, to compress the ensembled encoder into a new, more generalized single encoder. Extensive experiments on three benchmark datasets are presented, demonstrating SEDEG's superior performance compared to existing methods, especially in low-memory settings. Ablation studies are included to validate the effectiveness of the proposed components.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in its two-stage approach that sequentially enhances both the encoder and decoder generality in a class-incremental learning setting, particularly with small memory. Existing methods often focus on improving either the encoder *or* the decoder. The combination of feature boosting in the first stage with balanced KD and feature KD in the second is also a novel contribution. Further, addressing class imbalance in the context of KD for continual learning is a valuable aspect. The integration of these techniques into the DyTox architecture represents an incremental but non-trivial advance.

* **Significance:** Class-incremental learning is a significant problem in AI, as it mirrors how humans learn. Addressing catastrophic forgetting, especially with constrained resources (like small memory), has practical implications for deploying AI systems in real-world, dynamic environments. The superior performance of SEDEG, as demonstrated in the experiments, indicates its potential to improve the adaptability and robustness of continual learning models. The ablation studies strengthen the claims by isolating the contributions of different components.

* **Strengths:**
    * The two-stage approach is well-motivated and clearly explained.
    * The integration of feature boosting and knowledge distillation is effective.
    * The focus on small-memory scenarios is particularly relevant.
    * The experimental results are comprehensive, covering multiple datasets and settings.
    * Ablation studies provide strong evidence for the efficacy of individual components.
    * Addressing class imbalance during KD transfer is a valuable consideration.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * While SEDEG outperforms existing methods, the improvement magnitude varies across different datasets and configurations. Further analysis of *why* SEDEG excels in some situations more than others could strengthen the paper.
    * The method builds upon DyTox. While the extensions are significant, the reliance on this existing architecture might limit the perceived novelty for some. It is an incremental extension not a revolutionary approach.
    * The experiments focus on relatively standard benchmark datasets. Evaluating SEDEG on more challenging, real-world datasets could further validate its effectiveness.
    * While the code is available, a more detailed discussion on the computational cost of the ensembling approach and the subsequent KD would be valuable for practical adoption.

* **Potential Influence:**  The paper's approach has the potential to influence the field by:
    * Encouraging more research into enhancing both encoders and decoders in continual learning.
    * Promoting the use of feature boosting and balanced KD for improved knowledge transfer.
    * Highlighting the importance of addressing class imbalance in continual learning settings, especially with small memory.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed approach with clear improvements over existing methods for class-incremental learning. Its focus on small-memory scenarios increases its practical relevance.  While it builds upon existing architectures (DyTox) and techniques (feature boosting, knowledge distillation), the specific combination and adaptation for continual learning, along with the targeted approach to both the encoder and decoder, justifies a positive assessment.  The solid experimental results and ablation studies further support its contributions. Considering both the novelty and significance, I assign the following score:

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Breaking Reward Collapse: Adaptive Reinforcement for Open-ended Medical Reasoning with Enhanced Semantic Discrimination
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12957v1)
- **Authors**: Yizhou Liu, Jingwei Wei, Zizhi Chen, Minghao Han, Xukun Zhang, Keliang Liu, Lihua Zhang
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement learning (RL) with rule-based rewards has demonstrated strong potential in enhancing the reasoning and generalization capabilities of vision-language models (VLMs) and large language models (LLMs), while reducing computational overhead. However, its application in medical imaging remains underexplored. Existing reinforcement fine-tuning (RFT) approaches in this domain primarily target closed-ended visual question answering (VQA), limiting their applicability to real-world clinical reasoning. In contrast, open-ended medical VQA better reflects clinical practice but has received limited attention. While some efforts have sought to unify both formats via semantically guided RL, we observe that model-based semantic rewards often suffer from reward collapse, where responses with significant semantic differences receive similar scores. To address this, we propose ARMed (Adaptive Reinforcement for Medical Reasoning), a novel RL framework for open-ended medical VQA. ARMed first incorporates domain knowledge through supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on chain-of-thought data, then applies reinforcement learning with textual correctness and adaptive semantic rewards to enhance reasoning quality. We evaluate ARMed on six challenging medical VQA benchmarks. Results show that ARMed consistently boosts both accuracy and generalization, achieving a 32.64% improvement on in-domain tasks and an 11.65% gain on out-of-domain benchmarks. These results highlight the critical role of reward discriminability in medical RL and the promise of semantically guided rewards for enabling robust and clinically meaningful multimodal reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of "reward collapse" in reinforcement learning (RL) applied to open-ended medical Visual Question Answering (VQA). The authors observe that existing semantic reward metrics often fail to distinguish between responses with significant semantic differences, hindering effective learning. To counter this, they propose ARMed (Adaptive Reinforcement for Medical Reasoning), an RL framework that combines domain knowledge through supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on chain-of-thought data with RL using adaptive semantic rewards. The adaptive reward dynamically adjusts reward strength based on inter-sample variance, improving reward discriminability. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of ARMed on six medical VQA benchmarks, showing improvements in both accuracy and generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using adaptive semantic rewards to address reward collapse in the context of medical VQA is reasonably novel. While adaptive reward shaping has been explored in other RL settings, its application to open-ended medical VQA with semantic-based rewards and GRPO is a specific and worthwhile contribution. The identification and formalization of the reward collapse issue in the context of static semantic reward schemes is valuable. Medical thinking injection for addressing the model to repeat previously rewarded answer is another novel step.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a real-world problem with practical implications for clinical reasoning. By enhancing the accuracy and generalization capabilities of VQA systems, ARMed has the potential to improve clinical decision-making support tools. The improvements shown on the challenging medical VQA benchmarks, especially the out-of-domain ones, suggest that ARMed can lead to more robust and clinically relevant models. The focus on open-ended medical VQA is also significant because it aligns more closely with real-world clinical scenarios compared to multiple-choice tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies and articulates a specific problem (reward collapse) in medical VQA.
    *   The proposed ARMed framework is well-motivated and addresses the identified problem directly.
    *   The experimental results are compelling, showing consistent improvements across multiple benchmarks.
    *   The paper is well-written and presents a clear and logical argument.
    *   The method is compared against strong baselines, including both general and domain-specific VLMs, and the experiments show that ARMed achieves state-of-the-art results on both in-domain and out-of-domain medical VQA benchmarks.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on chain-of-thought data for the knowledge injection step introduces a dependence on the availability of such data. While the authors leverage this data to improve the reasoning capabilities of the model, it's not clear how well ARMed would perform without it.
    *   The complexity of the ARMed framework (three-stage training process with multiple components) might make it difficult to implement and fine-tune in practice.
    *   While the authors provide some justification for their design choices, further analysis of the impact of each component of ARMed would be beneficial. For example, a more detailed ablation study could shed light on the relative contributions of the adaptive reward, knowledge injection, and GRPO.
    *   The evaluation metrics rely on lexical overlap and semantic similarity metrics, which are not perfect proxies for clinical accuracy and meaningful reasoning. Expert evaluation by clinicians could further validate the clinical relevance of the improvements achieved by ARMed.
    *  The novelty is primarily an adaptation of existing RL techniques, albeit with significant engineering, to a specific domain.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future RL frameworks for medical VQA and other multimodal reasoning tasks. The adaptive reward mechanism could be adopted by other researchers to address reward collapse in similar settings. The emphasis on open-ended VQA could also encourage more research in this area, which is more aligned with real-world clinical practice. The paper may inspire related work on improving reward design, especially in tasks with complex or nuanced semantics.
    * The use of GRPO, and the overall technical approach may be too complex for easy adoption.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a solid understanding of the challenges in medical VQA, proposes a novel solution to a specific problem (reward collapse), and provides convincing experimental results. The use of adaptive semantic rewards and medical thinking knowledge injection represent a meaningful contribution to the field. However, the dependence on specific types of data, the complexity of the framework, and the limitations of the evaluation metrics limit its impact. The paper showcases a strong engineering effort. The conceptual contribution, however, is somewhat more incremental. For these reasons, a strong but not exceptional score is warranted.

### Compact Attention: Exploiting Structured Spatio-Temporal Sparsity for Fast Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12969v1)
- **Authors**: Qirui Li, Guangcong Zheng, Qi Zhao, Jie Li, Bin Dong, Yiwu Yao, Xi Li
- **Abstract**: The computational demands of self-attention mechanisms pose a critical challenge for transformer-based video generation, particularly in synthesizing ultra-long sequences. Current approaches, such as factorized attention and fixed sparse patterns, fail to fully exploit the inherent spatio-temporal redundancies in video data. Through systematic analysis of video diffusion transformers (DiT), we uncover a key insight: Attention matrices exhibit structured, yet heterogeneous sparsity patterns, where specialized heads dynamically attend to distinct spatiotemporal regions (e.g., local pattern, cross-shaped pattern, or global pattern). Existing sparse attention methods either impose rigid constraints or introduce significant overhead, limiting their effectiveness. To address this, we propose Compact Attention, a hardware-aware acceleration framework featuring three innovations: 1) Adaptive tiling strategies that approximate diverse spatial interaction patterns via dynamic tile grouping, 2) Temporally varying windows that adjust sparsity levels based on frame proximity, and 3) An automated configuration search algorithm that optimizes sparse patterns while preserving critical attention pathways. Our method achieves 1.6~2.5x acceleration in attention computation on single-GPU setups while maintaining comparable visual quality with full-attention baselines. This work provides a principled approach to unlocking efficient long-form video generation through structured sparsity exploitation. Project Page: https://yo-ava.github.io/Compact-Attention.github.io/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Compact Attention: Exploiting Structured Spatio-Temporal Sparsity for Fast Video Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the computational bottleneck of self-attention in transformer-based video generation, particularly for long sequences. The authors analyze video diffusion transformers (DiT) and observe structured sparsity in attention matrices, where different attention heads focus on specific spatio-temporal regions. They propose "Compact Attention," a hardware-aware acceleration framework with three main components: 1) adaptive tiling to approximate diverse spatial interaction patterns, 2) temporally varying windows adjusting sparsity based on frame proximity, and 3) an automated configuration search to optimize sparse patterns while preserving crucial attention pathways. The method aims to achieve faster attention computation without sacrificing visual quality. Experimental results on Wan2.1 and Hunyuan models demonstrate speedups of 1.6x to 2.5x with comparable visual quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a valuable synthesis of existing techniques and novel insights. The observation of structured yet heterogeneous sparsity within DiT attention matrices is a key contribution. Leveraging structured sparsity for efficient video generation isn't entirely new, but the *specific combination* of adaptive tiling, temporally varying windows, and automated configuration search is innovative. Furthermore, the focus on hardware-aware acceleration sets it apart from purely algorithmic approaches. The authors carefully contrast it with Sliding Window Attention, Sparse VideoGen, and SpargeAttn. However, some of the individual components may draw inspiration from related work in image and language domains, even though they're adapted for video.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a major bottleneck in video generation: the computational cost of self-attention. By achieving significant speedups with minimal quality degradation, it paves the way for more efficient generation of ultra-long videos. This has implications for various applications, including content creation, video editing, and real-time streaming. The insights into attention patterns within video transformers could also inform future model architectures and training strategies. The empirical validation on SOTA models (Wan2.1 & Hunyuan) and comprehensive comparison to baselines strengthens its significance.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Systematic analysis of attention patterns in video diffusion transformers.
    *   Well-designed framework with innovative components.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation with comparisons to relevant baselines and ablation studies.
    *   Focus on hardware-aware optimization, increasing its practical relevance.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the approach is training-free it still relies on an offline search to define the sparse mask. This is computationally expensive and may limit the ease of adoption.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of the trade-offs between sparsity, speed, and visual quality, and how these are influenced by the different components of the framework.
    *   The lack of generalization on different hardware architectures (other than H800) limits the broader impact of the proposed technique.
    *   The writing, although clear, could be more concise.

**Justification:**

Overall, the paper makes a solid contribution to the field of video generation. While the novelty lies more in the combination and adaptation of techniques than in groundbreaking individual components, the practical significance of achieving substantial speedups in long-form video generation is undeniable. The insights into attention patterns and the hardware-aware design are also valuable. The weaknesses mentioned prevent it from being an exceptional contribution, but it's a well-executed and impactful piece of work.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Matrix-Game 2.0: An Open-Source, Real-Time, and Streaming Interactive World Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13009v1)
- **Authors**: Xianglong He, Chunli Peng, Zexiang Liu, Boyang Wang, Yifan Zhang, Qi Cui, Fei Kang, Biao Jiang, Mengyin An, Yangyang Ren, Baixin Xu, Hao-Xiang Guo, Kaixiong Gong, Cyrus Wu, Wei Li, Xuchen Song, Yang Liu, Eric Li, Yahui Zhou
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in interactive video generations have demonstrated diffusion model's potential as world models by capturing complex physical dynamics and interactive behaviors. However, existing interactive world models depend on bidirectional attention and lengthy inference steps, severely limiting real-time performance. Consequently, they are hard to simulate real-world dynamics, where outcomes must update instantaneously based on historical context and current actions. To address this, we present Matrix-Game 2.0, an interactive world model generates long videos on-the-fly via few-step auto-regressive diffusion. Our framework consists of three key components: (1) A scalable data production pipeline for Unreal Engine and GTA5 environments to effectively produce massive amounts (about 1200 hours) of video data with diverse interaction annotations; (2) An action injection module that enables frame-level mouse and keyboard inputs as interactive conditions; (3) A few-step distillation based on the casual architecture for real-time and streaming video generation. Matrix Game 2.0 can generate high-quality minute-level videos across diverse scenes at an ultra-fast speed of 25 FPS. We open-source our model weights and codebase to advance research in interactive world modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Matrix-Game 2.0: An Open-Source, Real-Time, and Streaming Interactive World Model":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Matrix-Game 2.0, an interactive world model designed for real-time video generation.  It addresses the limitations of existing interactive video generation models, particularly their inability to perform in real-time due to bidirectional attention and computationally expensive inference. Matrix-Game 2.0 achieves real-time performance using a few-step auto-regressive diffusion model. The system consists of: (1) a scalable data production pipeline using Unreal Engine and GTA5 for generating large-scale interactive video datasets; (2) an action injection module to incorporate mouse and keyboard inputs as interactive conditions; and (3) few-step distillation based on causal architectures for real-time generation. The authors claim to achieve 25 FPS video generation at minute-level quality and release their model and code to foster further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The paper builds upon existing techniques like diffusion models and auto-regressive architectures. However, the specific combination of these elements, along with the focus on real-time interactive performance, brings a degree of novelty. The data generation pipeline is a significant contribution, addressing a known bottleneck in the field: the scarcity of high-quality, annotated interactive video datasets.

    *   The use of Self-Forcing for distillation is a well-established technique, but its specific application and optimization within the Matrix-Game 2.0 architecture are tailored for real-time performance.
*   **Significance:**

    *   The primary significance lies in addressing the real-time performance bottleneck in interactive video generation. The ability to generate long, interactive videos at 25 FPS has substantial implications for applications like gaming, virtual reality, and robotics simulation. The open-source release is another significant contribution, enabling other researchers to build upon their work.

    *   The comprehensive data pipeline is potentially a significant advance, given the challenges in creating training datasets with precise actions, camera controls, and diverse in-game interactions. This aspect is particularly important for learning accurate and realistic world models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Real-time Performance:** The most significant strength is achieving real-time (25 FPS) video generation. This is a critical step towards practical interactive applications.

    *   **Data Pipeline:** The design and implementation of a scalable data production pipeline in Unreal Engine and GTA5 environments. The accurate capture of interactive data is often a challenge. This part is a huge contribution for future researches.

    *   **Open-Source:** The open-sourcing of the model weights and code base will undoubtedly accelerate research in this area.

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper uses multi-dimensional evaluation framework examining four critical capabilities: visual quality, temporal quality, action controllability and physical rule understanding, which could be helpful in assessing future research works.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Established Techniques:** The core of the model relies on existing diffusion models and auto-regressive architectures. While the engineering optimizations are significant, the fundamental algorithmic contribution is limited.

    *   **Resolution:** The output resolution (352x640) is relatively low compared to modern video generation models. This limits the visual fidelity and realism of the generated videos.

    *   **Generalization Issues:** The paper acknowledges generalization issues, particularly with "out-of-domain" scenes, indicating the model might struggle with scenarios significantly different from its training data. While this is expected in such models, the severity of these issues reduces the practical use cases.

    *   **Limited Novelty in Distillation:** The use of Self-Forcing for distillation, while effective, isn't a novel technique. It's more of an application and optimization of an existing method.
* **Overall:**

While the paper builds heavily on existing techniques, it tackles a very important problem: real-time interactive video generation. The engineering efforts to create the data pipeline and optimize the system for speed are substantial. The open-source release further enhances its significance. The primary limitations are its resolution and generalization capability, which affects practical uses. Overall, the paper presents a useful set of engineering innovations along with a data production framework which will significantly benefit the interactive video generation.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The score reflects a strong engineering contribution with a limited algorithmic novelty. Achieving real-time performance and addressing the data scarcity problem are highly valuable. The limitations in resolution and generalization hold it back from being a truly breakthrough paper. The overall contribution has merit in improving the feasibility of interactive video generation which is an important research direction.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PC-Sampler: Position-Aware Calibration of Decoding Bias in Masked Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13021v1)
- **Authors**: Pengcheng Huang, Shuhao Liu, Zhenghao Liu, Yukun Yan, Shuo Wang, Zulong Chen, Tong Xiao
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in masked diffusion models (MDMs) have established them as powerful non-autoregressive alternatives for sequence generation. Nevertheless, our preliminary experiments reveal that the generation quality of MDMs is still highly sensitive to the choice of decoding strategy. In particular, widely adopted uncertainty-based samplers suffer from two key limitations: a lack of global trajectory control and a pronounced bias toward trivial tokens in the early stages of decoding. These shortcomings restrict the full potential of MDMs. In this work, we introduce Position-Aware Confidence-Calibrated Sampling (PC-Sampler), a novel decoding strategy that unifies global trajectory planning with content-aware informativeness maximization. PC-Sampler incorporates a position-aware weighting mechanism to regulate the decoding path and a calibrated confidence score to suppress the premature selection of trivial tokens. Extensive experiments on three advanced MDMs across seven challenging benchmarks-including logical reasoning and planning tasks-demonstrate that PC-Sampler consistently outperforms existing MDM decoding strategies by more than 10% on average, significantly narrowing the performance gap with state-of-the-art autoregressive models. All codes are available at https://github.com/NEUIR/PC-Sampler.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "PC-Sampler: Position-Aware Calibration of Decoding Bias in Masked Diffusion Models" addresses limitations in uncertainty-based decoding strategies for Masked Diffusion Models (MDMs). It identifies two key issues: a lack of global trajectory control (leading to a U-shaped decoding order) and a bias toward trivial tokens (like punctuation) in early decoding stages.
