To overcome these problems, the authors introduce PC-Sampler, a novel decoding strategy that combines global trajectory planning (via a position-aware weighting mechanism) and content-aware informativeness maximization (via a frequency-calibrated confidence score). Experiments on seven diverse benchmarks using three advanced MDMs demonstrate that PC-Sampler consistently outperforms existing decoding strategies, narrowing the performance gap with state-of-the-art autoregressive models. Furthermore, it can be combined with efficient decoding methods for faster inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its combined approach to addressing the two identified limitations of uncertainty-based sampling. While position-aware decoding and confidence calibration have been explored separately in other contexts, the paper's specific formulation within the MDM framework and the integration of both aspects are novel. The discovery and articulation of the U-shaped decoding trajectory and trivial token bias provide important insights.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is potentially high. MDMs are becoming increasingly important as non-autoregressive alternatives for sequence generation, and improving their decoding strategies is crucial for unlocking their full potential. Overcoming the U-shaped decoding trajectory and trivial token bias can lead to more coherent and informative generation, as shown by the experimental results. Furthermore, the ability to combine PC-Sampler with efficient decoding techniques makes it practically relevant. The experimental results, especially those showing PC-Sampler narrowing the gap with or even surpassing autoregressive models, are quite compelling.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly articulated problem: The paper clearly identifies and explains the limitations of existing decoding strategies.
    *   Well-designed approach: PC-Sampler provides an intuitive and well-motivated solution.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation: Experiments are conducted on a diverse set of tasks and models.
    *   Strong results: The results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of PC-Sampler.
    *   Ablation studies: The ablation studies offer insights into the contribution of individual components.
    *   Compatibility: Demonstrates PC-Sampler can be combined with other existing accelerating mechanisms.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the concepts behind the approach are well described, the presentation is somewhat dense. More illustrative examples would enhance understanding.
    *   The dependence on hyperparameters (λ, α) might limit the approach's broader applicability. While the study discusses how to tune these, it would be better if a principled way of choosing hyperparameters adaptively were presented or discovered.
    *   The paper relies on specific MDM architectures, which may make the findings difficult to generalize.

**Justification for Score:**

PC-Sampler addresses a practical problem in MDM decoding and offers a well-designed and demonstrably effective solution. While the components are not entirely groundbreaking on their own, the way they're combined, justified within the MDM setting, and the resulting performance improvements makes it a notable contribution. The systematic analysis of existing problems and their causes adds value to the research. The approach could have limited generalizability due to hyperparameter dependance and reliance on existing architecture. Therefore, I am assigning a score of:

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### G$^2$RPO-A: Guided Group Relative Policy Optimization with Adaptive Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13023v1)
- **Authors**: Yongxin Guo, Wenbo Deng, Zhenglin Cheng, Xiaoying Tang
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) has markedly enhanced the reasoning abilities of large language models (LLMs). Its success, however, largely depends on strong base models with rich world knowledge, yielding only modest improvements for small-size language models (SLMs). To address this limitation, we investigate Guided GRPO, which injects ground-truth reasoning steps into roll-out trajectories to compensate for SLMs' inherent weaknesses. Through a comprehensive study of various guidance configurations, we find that naively adding guidance delivers limited gains. These insights motivate G$^2$RPO-A, an adaptive algorithm that automatically adjusts guidance strength in response to the model's evolving training dynamics. Experiments on mathematical reasoning and code-generation benchmarks confirm that G$^2$RPO-A substantially outperforms vanilla GRPO. Our code and models are available at https://github.com/T-Lab-CUHKSZ/G2RPO-A.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Guided Group Relative Policy Optimization with Adaptive Guidance (G2RPO-A), an algorithm designed to improve the reasoning abilities of small language models (SLMs) within a Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) framework. The core idea is to inject ground-truth reasoning steps (guidance) into roll-out trajectories to compensate for the inherent weaknesses of SLMs. The paper identifies that naive guidance provides limited gains and, therefore, proposes an adaptive approach (G2RPO-A) that automatically adjusts the guidance strength based on the model's training dynamics. Experimental results on mathematical reasoning and code generation benchmarks demonstrate that G2RPO-A outperforms vanilla GRPO.  A curriculum learning strategy is also incorporated to address sample difficulty.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its adaptive guidance mechanism for GRPO, specifically tailored for SLMs. While the general idea of using guidance in RL is not new, the paper's systematic study of different guidance configurations (ratio, length) and its automatic adjustment based on training dynamics is a significant contribution. The analysis of hard training samples using curriculum learning further enhances the novelty of the approach. Compared to concurrent work, G2RPO-A goes beyond simply adding guidance tokens, offering a systematic analysis and adaptive mechanism.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation of GRPO, namely its poor scalability to smaller models. The improvement in performance for SLMs opens up the possibility of using RLVR techniques in resource-constrained environments. The comprehensive analysis of guidance configurations provides valuable insights for future research in this area. By making RLVR more effective for smaller models, this work has the potential to broaden the applicability of this powerful technique. This is especially important as smaller models become increasingly relevant due to their efficiency and deployability in edge devices.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Investigation:** The paper performs a detailed analysis of various guidance parameters (ratio, length) and their impact on performance.
    *   **Adaptive Mechanism:** The proposed adaptive guidance mechanism is well-motivated and provides a practical solution to the challenges of using guidance effectively in RLVR.
    *   **Experimental Validation:**  The paper presents strong experimental results on multiple benchmarks (mathematical reasoning and code generation) with different models.
    *   **Curriculum Learning Integration:**  Incorporating curriculum learning addresses issues with sample difficulty and contributes to the overall robustness of the method.
    *   **Comparison to Baselines:** Demonstrating clear improvements over vanilla GRPO and simple guided baselines strengthens the contributions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Model Architectures:** While the paper considers several model families within the Qwen series, the evaluation could be strengthened by including a more diverse set of model architectures to demonstrate the generalizability of the approach.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The paper discusses the importance of guidance parameters but doesn't fully address the potential sensitivity to the hyperparameters of the adaptive guidance mechanism. This might require users to perform extensive tuning for new tasks or models.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Though ablations are performed over guidance ratio and length, the components of the adaptive mechanism itself are not fully ablated, which could further reveal the relative importance of each part.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in the following ways:
    *   **Guidance strategies in RLVR:** The adaptive guidance mechanism could be adopted and extended by other researchers working on RLVR.
    *   **Scalability of RLVR:** The focus on SLMs could encourage more research on making RLVR techniques more accessible and applicable to smaller models.
    *   **Curriculum Learning in RLVR:** Integrating curriculum learning with RLVR for SLMs could inspire new approaches to address sample difficulty and improve training efficiency.

*Score:* 7.5

*Rationale:* While the idea of using guidance in RL isn't entirely new, the paper's specific contribution – the adaptive guidance mechanism tailored for SLMs in the GRPO framework, combined with its systematic evaluation and integration of curriculum learning – is a solid contribution. It addresses a significant limitation in existing GRPO approaches and shows clear empirical improvements. The weaknesses, particularly the lack of architectural diversity in models tested and potential hyperparameter sensitivity, prevent a higher score. The adaptive element also needs to demonstrate an even better capability to generalize across new task domains, which is not yet fully evident. Nevertheless, the work provides valuable insights and has the potential to influence future research on RLVR and SLMs, justifying a score of 7.5. The detailed analysis of guidance parameters offers a significant contribution to future works in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Application of Transformer-Based Models for Predicting Consequences of Cyber Attacks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13030v1)
- **Authors**: Bipin Chhetri, Akbar Siami Namin
- **Abstract**: Cyberattacks are increasing, and securing against such threats is costing industries billions of dollars annually. Threat Modeling, that is, comprehending the consequences of these attacks, can provide critical support to cybersecurity professionals, enabling them to take timely action and allocate resources that could be used elsewhere. Cybersecurity is heavily dependent on threat modeling, as it assists security experts in assessing and mitigating risks related to identifying vulnerabilities and threats. Recently, there has been a pressing need for automated methods to assess attack descriptions and forecast the future consequences of the increasing complexity of cyberattacks. This study examines how Natural Language Processing (NLP) and deep learning can be applied to analyze the potential impact of cyberattacks by leveraging textual descriptions from the MITRE Common Weakness Enumeration (CWE) database. We emphasize classifying attack consequences into five principal categories: Availability, Access Control, Confidentiality, Integrity, and Other. This paper investigates the use of Bidirectional Encoder Representations from Transformers (BERT) in combination with Hierarchical Attention Networks (HANs) for Multi-label classification, evaluating their performance in comparison with conventional CNN and LSTM-based models. Experimental findings show that BERT achieves an overall accuracy of $0.972$, far higher than conventional deep learning models in multi-label classification. HAN outperforms baseline forms of CNN and LSTM-based models on specific cybersecurity labels. However, BERT consistently achieves better precision and recall, making it more suitable for predicting the consequences of a cyberattack.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the application of transformer-based models, specifically BERT and Hierarchical Attention Networks (HAN), for predicting the consequences of cyberattacks. It uses the MITRE CWE database to classify attack consequences into five categories: Availability, Access Control, Confidentiality, Integrity, and Other. The study compares the performance of BERT and HAN against traditional CNN and LSTM-based models for multi-label classification. The results show that BERT significantly outperforms the other models in overall accuracy and F1-score, while HAN shows some strengths in specific label prediction.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper addresses a well-defined and important problem in cybersecurity: threat modeling and predicting the consequences of attacks.
    *   **Rigorous Experimentation:** The study includes a clear description of the models, datasets, preprocessing steps, and evaluation metrics.  The performance comparison against CNN and LSTM baselines is valuable.
    *   **Strong Results:** The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of BERT for this multi-label classification task, achieving significantly higher accuracy and F1-scores than the baseline models. The quantitative analysis is convincing.
    *   **Well Structured:** The paper is logically organized and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the methodology and results.
    *  **Practical Impact:** Addresses an important question in a field with enormous economic implications.

* **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty:** While the application of BERT to this specific problem is useful, it isn't fundamentally novel. BERT has been applied to many NLP tasks, and this study represents a relatively straightforward adaptation.
    *   **HAN's Limited Success:** The HAN model, while presented as a main component, doesn't perform as well as BERT. The discussion of its strengths in certain labels is somewhat overshadowed by BERT's overall dominance.  More analysis of why HAN underperforms would be beneficial.
    *   **Dataset Limitations:** The paper relies on the MITRE CWE dataset, which, while a standard resource, may have inherent biases or limitations. Further discussion of these potential biases would be beneficial. The imbalance of the dataset's labels isn't fully addressed (though mentioned in the conclusion).  Data augmentation techniques are suggested for future work, but this could have been explored during the current study.
    *   **Lack of Ablation Studies:** The paper doesn't include ablation studies to understand the impact of different components of the BERT model (e.g., removing attention heads).  This would provide deeper insights into why BERT is effective.
    *   **Limited Comparison to Similar Works:** The related work section could be expanded.  While it discusses related studies, it could benefit from a more detailed comparison of methodologies and results from other studies that have used machine learning to analyze cyberattack data.

* **Significance:**

    *   The paper's significance lies in demonstrating the practical applicability of a powerful NLP model (BERT) to a real-world cybersecurity problem. This can encourage further adoption of such models in cybersecurity and potentially lead to more accurate and efficient threat modeling.
    *   The study highlights the benefits of using transformer-based models for tasks involving complex textual data, particularly when domain-specific knowledge and terminology are important.

* **Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a solid application of BERT to a relevant problem in cybersecurity. The results are clear and the analysis is well-conducted. However, the lack of fundamental novelty and some limitations in the experimental setup and analysis prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. It is more of an evolutionary step than a revolutionary one.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:**

A score of 6 reflects the following:

*   The research is well-executed and provides valuable insights into using BERT for predicting cyberattack consequences. (Positive)
*   The novelty is limited; it mainly applies a known technique to a specific dataset. (Negative)
*   The dataset imbalance is acknowledged but not fully addressed in the current study. (Negative)
*   The lack of ablation studies hinders a deeper understanding of the model's behavior. (Negative)
*  HAN performance isn't adequately explained (Negative)
*   The paper can serve as a useful reference point for other researchers and practitioners interested in applying NLP to cybersecurity. (Positive)
*   The study serves a very important question within the field. (Positive)

The score acknowledges the value of the work while also recognizing its limitations in terms of originality and the depth of analysis.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Can Large Models Teach Student Models to Solve Mathematical Problems Like Human Beings? A Reasoning Distillation Method via Multi-LoRA Interaction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13037v1)
- **Authors**: Xinhe Li, Jiajun Liu, Peng Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent studies have demonstrated that Large Language Models (LLMs) have strong mathematical reasoning abilities but rely on hundreds of billions of parameters. To tackle the challenge of poor reasoning in Small Language Models (SLMs), existing methods typically leverage LLMs to generate massive amounts of data for cramming training. In psychology, they are akin to System 1 thinking, which resolves reasoning problems rapidly based on experience and intuition. However, human learning also requires System 2 thinking, where knowledge is first acquired and then reinforced through practice. Inspired by such two distinct modes of thinking, we propose a novel method based on the multi-LoRA Interaction for mathematical reasoning Distillation (LoRID). First, we input the question and reasoning of each sample into an LLM to create knowledge-enhanced datasets. Subsequently, we train a LoRA block on the student model as an Intuitive Reasoner (IR), which directly generates Chain-of-Thoughts for problem-solving. Then, to imitate System 2 thinking, we train the Knowledge Generator (KG) and Deep Reasoner (DR), respectively. The former outputs only knowledge after receiving problems, while the latter uses that knowledge to perform reasoning. Finally, to address the randomness in the generation of IR and DR, we evaluate whether their outputs are consistent, and the inference process needs to be iterated if not. This step can enhance the mathematical reasoning ability of SLMs through mutual feedback. Experimental results show that LoRID achieves state-of-the-art performance, especially on the GSM8K dataset, where it outperforms the second-best method by 2.3%, 16.1%, 2.4%, 12.3%, and 1.8% accuracy across the five base models, respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LoRID, a novel method for distilling mathematical reasoning abilities from large language models (LLMs) into smaller language models (SLMs). LoRID is inspired by the System 1 and System 2 thinking framework in psychology. The method involves three LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) blocks on the SLM: an Intuitive Reasoner (IR) for quick, intuitive reasoning (System 1), a Knowledge Generator (KG) for extracting relevant knowledge from the LLM (akin to teaching), and a Deep Reasoner (DR) for applying the extracted knowledge to solve problems (System 2). A multi-LoRA interaction mechanism is used to iteratively refine the solutions by checking the consistency between the outputs of IR and DR. If inconsistencies exist, the modules iterate, effectively providing feedback.  Experiments on GSM8K and MATH datasets demonstrate that LoRID achieves state-of-the-art performance, outperforming existing methods and showing significant improvements in reasoning accuracy across various base models. The method can also be easily integrated with existing CoT distillation methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the combination of insights from cognitive psychology with a LoRA-based distillation approach. Distilling reasoning capabilities is a well-explored area, but the explicit attempt to mimic the dual-system cognitive process (System 1 and System 2 thinking) and the LoRA interaction is novel.  The knowledge generation part also presents a different way to augment training data which focuses on teaching basic rules instead of more examples. Compared to existing approaches which mainly focuses on data augmentation, LoRID proposes a more structured teaching and learning approach for math reasoning.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge: enabling SLMs to perform complex reasoning tasks without incurring the computational costs associated with large models. The significant performance gains on standard benchmarks, especially GSM8K, underscore the potential of LoRID to improve the mathematical reasoning capabilities of SLMs. The plug-and-play nature of LoRA blocks further enhances its practicality. By enabling resource-efficient reasoning in smaller models, LoRID can make advanced NLP capabilities more accessible and deployable in real-world scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Performance:** The experimental results consistently demonstrate the effectiveness of LoRID across different base models and datasets, especially on GSM8K.
    *   **Clear Motivation:** The paper provides a clear and compelling rationale for the approach, drawing on cognitive psychology to motivate the design choices.
    *   **Parameter Efficiency:** LoRA-based implementation ensures that the method is parameter-efficient.
    *   **Integration Capability:** The modular design of LoRID allows it to be easily integrated into existing CoT distillation methods.
    *   **Detailed Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies clearly show the importance of both System 1, System 2 and the multi-LoRA interaction.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on LLMs for Knowledge Generation:** The method still relies on powerful closed-source LLMs (GPT-4) for knowledge generation. While the distilled model is small, the initial knowledge acquisition step requires access to a large model. This could limit its accessibility for researchers who don't have access to such models.
    *   **Limited Generalization on MATH Dataset:** The improvements on the MATH dataset, while present, are not as dramatic as those on GSM8K. The paper attributes this to the advantage of tool-based methods in complex calculations, but a more thorough analysis of this discrepancy would be beneficial.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** While the approach is inspired by cognitive psychology, the paper lacks a more formal theoretical justification for why this specific combination of LoRA blocks and interaction mechanism works.
    *   **Ablation study in Tab.3 lacks diversity.** Table 3 only shows a limited set of comparisons given a larger number of experiments. Including more related methods would provide deeper insights into the advantages and drawbacks of the proposed method.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several areas:
    *   **Distillation Methods:** The LoRID framework offers a new perspective on distillation, moving beyond simple data augmentation to incorporate more structured teaching and learning principles.
    *   **Reasoning in SLMs:** The method can inspire further research into techniques for enhancing reasoning capabilities in smaller language models.
    *   **Cognitive-Inspired NLP:** The paper demonstrates the value of drawing inspiration from cognitive psychology to design more effective NLP models.

*   **Justification of Score:**
    Given the novelty in combining cognitive insights with LoRA-based distillation, the significant performance gains on GSM8K, and the potential to influence future research, the paper warrants a solid score. However, the reliance on LLMs for knowledge generation, the weaker results on the MATH dataset, and the lack of a more formal theoretical analysis prevent it from achieving a higher score.

**Score: 7.8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Büyük Dil Modelleri için TR-MMLU Benchmarkı: Performans Değerlendirmesi, Zorluklar ve İyileştirme Fırsatları
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13044v1)
- **Authors**: M. Ali Bayram, Ali Arda Fincan, Ahmet Semih Gümüş, Banu Diri, Savaş Yıldırım, Öner Aytaş
- **Abstract**: Language models have made significant advancements in understanding and generating human language, achieving remarkable success in various applications. However, evaluating these models remains a challenge, particularly for resource-limited languages like Turkish. To address this issue, we introduce the Turkish MMLU (TR-MMLU) benchmark, a comprehensive evaluation framework designed to assess the linguistic and conceptual capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in Turkish. TR-MMLU is based on a meticulously curated dataset comprising 6,200 multiple-choice questions across 62 sections within the Turkish education system. This benchmark provides a standard framework for Turkish NLP research, enabling detailed analyses of LLMs' capabilities in processing Turkish text. In this study, we evaluated state-of-the-art LLMs on TR-MMLU, highlighting areas for improvement in model design. TR-MMLU sets a new standard for advancing Turkish NLP research and inspiring future innovations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TR-MMLU, a new benchmark for evaluating the performance of large language models (LLMs) in the Turkish language. TR-MMLU consists of 6,200 multiple-choice questions across 62 categories, sourced from the Turkish education system. The authors argue that existing benchmarks are insufficient for assessing LLMs' ability to understand and process Turkish due to its unique linguistic features and cultural context. The paper details the creation of the dataset, the evaluation methodology, and the results of evaluating 39 different LLMs (both open-source and proprietary) on the benchmark. The results highlight the challenges posed by Turkish morphology and the need for improved tokenization strategies and fine-tuning methods. The paper also advocates for expanding TR-MMLU to include open-ended tasks and other NLP tasks to provide a more comprehensive evaluation. Finally, the paper is published on Hugging Face platform as three separate data sets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a gap:** The paper tackles a crucial problem: the lack of adequate benchmarks for evaluating LLMs in resource-limited languages like Turkish. This is a valuable contribution as most existing benchmarks are biased towards English.
*   **Culturally and linguistically relevant:**  The benchmark is specifically designed for Turkish, drawing from the Turkish education system, ensuring that the questions are culturally and linguistically appropriate. This mitigates issues of translation errors and cultural biases found in translated benchmarks.
*   **Comprehensive dataset:** The TR-MMLU dataset is relatively large and covers a wide range of topics, offering a more holistic assessment of LLM capabilities.
*   **Rigorous evaluation:** The paper describes a well-defined evaluation methodology, including the use of controlled hardware environments, multiple prompts, paraphrase detection and a leaderboard.
*   **Publicly available:** The datasets and leaderboard are publicly available on Hugging Face which promotes transparency, reproducibility, and further research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited novelty in question type:** The benchmark relies primarily on multiple-choice questions. While this facilitates evaluation, it may not fully capture the depth of understanding or reasoning abilities of LLMs compared to more complex tasks like open-ended question answering or creative text generation. The authors acknowledge this, but the reliance on multiple-choice questions remains a limitation.
*   **Potential overlap with pre-training data:**  While the authors claim the dataset is carefully curated to avoid overlap with pre-training data, it's extremely difficult to completely eliminate the possibility, especially given the vast amount of data used to train modern LLMs. A more rigorous analysis of potential data contamination would strengthen the paper.
*   **Lack of in-depth error analysis:** While the paper presents overall performance metrics, a more detailed analysis of the types of errors made by different models would provide valuable insights into their specific weaknesses and inform future research directions. For example, are models struggling more with certain topics, linguistic constructs, or reasoning tasks?
*   **Limited scope of NLP tasks:** The benchmark is limited to evaluation of LLMs based on education related multiple choice questions. Expanding the scope beyond multiple choice tasks to include tasks like open-ended questions or sentiment analysis could lead to further understanding of LLMs abilities for different areas of NLP.

**Significance:**

The paper is significant in that it provides a much-needed resource for the Turkish NLP community. It provides a standardized framework for evaluating LLMs and identifying areas for improvement. The release of the dataset and leaderboard will likely stimulate further research and development of Turkish language models.

**Overall:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by addressing a critical need for Turkish language benchmarks. While it has some limitations, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses. The creation and public release of TR-MMLU represent a significant step forward for the Turkish NLP community.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

A score of 7 reflects the paper's clear contribution to the field. It's not groundbreaking research that fundamentally changes the landscape of NLP, and the reliance on multiple-choice questions and the lack of a detailed error analysis are limiting factors. However, the paper successfully introduces a culturally and linguistically appropriate benchmark that fills a significant gap, with robust evaluation methodology. The paper offers a good balance of rigour in method and significance of contribution to the relevant area of focus.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Using AI for User Representation: An Analysis of 83 Persona Prompts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13047v1)
- **Authors**: Joni Salminen, Danial Amin, Bernard Jansen
- **Abstract**: We analyzed 83 persona prompts from 27 research articles that used large language models (LLMs) to generate user personas. Findings show that the prompts predominantly generate single personas. Several prompts express a desire for short or concise persona descriptions, which deviates from the tradition of creating rich, informative, and rounded persona profiles. Text is the most common format for generated persona attributes, followed by numbers. Text and numbers are often generated together, and demographic attributes are included in nearly all generated personas. Researchers use up to 12 prompts in a single study, though most research uses a small number of prompts. Comparison and testing multiple LLMs is rare. More than half of the prompts require the persona output in a structured format, such as JSON, and 74% of the prompts insert data or dynamic variables. We discuss the implications of increased use of computational personas for user representation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents an analysis of 83 persona prompts from 27 research articles that leverage Large Language Models (LLMs) for generating user personas. It systematically examines *why* researchers use these prompts, *how* they use them, and *what kind* of personas are generated. The study reveals key trends, including the dominance of generating single personas, a desire for concise descriptions, the frequent use of structured outputs (like JSON), and the prevalence of demographic attributes.  It also highlights that the personas generated seem to be slightly less informative than earlier generations of computational personas. The research discusses the implications of these findings, particularly regarding transparency, human agency, and the potential for data-driven practices being superseded by LLM's "knowledge".  It calls for more research on evaluating the effects of individual prompts and complete persona generation systems, emphasizing the importance of understanding how prompt design impacts the output and overall usefulness of LLM-generated personas.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Approach:** The paper adopts a systematic methodology for extracting and analyzing persona prompts, enhancing the rigor of its findings. This systematic mapping fills a knowledge gap in the understanding of LLM's application in persona creation.
    *   **Timely and Relevant:** The topic is highly relevant given the rapid proliferation of LLMs in various fields, including HCI and user-centered design. Understanding how LLMs are being used for persona generation is crucial for developing best practices and mitigating potential risks.
    *   **Clear Research Questions:**  The research questions are well-defined and provide a clear focus for the analysis.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The analysis covers various aspects of persona prompts, including their purpose, structure, content, and the types of personas they generate. This thoroughness provides valuable insights into current practices.
    *   **Identifies Key Trends and Issues:** The paper successfully identifies important trends, such as the shift towards concise persona descriptions and the potential for LLMs to overshadow data-driven practices. The identification of how researchers want to control verbosity of LLMs is also interesting.
    *   **Practical Recommendations:**  The recommendations for maintaining data-drivenness and familiarizing oneself with persona theory are valuable for researchers and practitioners. The emphasis on human agency is welcome.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Impact Assessment:** The study focuses primarily on analyzing *prompts* and doesn't provide substantial empirical evidence on the *impact* of these prompts on the quality or effectiveness of the resulting personas or on downstream design decisions.  While it identifies trends, it doesn't deeply explore the consequences of these trends.
    *   **Lack of Causal Analysis:** The paper identifies correlations and associations, but it doesn't establish causal relationships between specific prompt characteristics and the quality of personas or user experiences.
    *   **Model-Centric View:** The analysis is largely model-centric, focusing on LLMs. Broader discussion regarding the impact on the 'user' or designer experience with computationally-generated personas would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited Discussion on Bias:** While the paper briefly mentions biases, a more in-depth discussion of the potential biases embedded in LLMs and how they might influence persona generation would strengthen the analysis.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The systematic analysis of persona prompts is novel and provides a valuable overview of current practices.
    *   The identification of key trends and issues in LLM-driven persona generation contributes to the existing body of knowledge and raises important questions for future research.
    *   The paper lays the groundwork for future research that investigates the effectiveness of different prompting strategies and their impact on persona quality and design outcomes.

**Justification for the Score:**

Despite its strengths, the paper's primary limitation is its lack of empirical assessment on the *impact* of different prompting strategies. While the analysis of prompts is valuable, the true significance lies in understanding how these prompts influence persona quality and design decisions. The study provides a descriptive analysis of what's happening, but it doesn't delve deeply into *why* certain practices are more or less effective or *what* the consequences of these practices are. The model-centric focus detracts from a richer perspective. Also the lack of causal analysis and limited discussion on bias slightly weaken the paper.

Therefore, while the paper makes a solid contribution to the field by systematically analyzing persona prompts, it falls short of being a highly impactful or transformative piece of research. The limitations discussed above prevent it from achieving a higher score.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### MAJIC: Markovian Adaptive Jailbreaking via Iterative Composition of Diverse Innovative Strategies
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13048v1)
- **Authors**: Weiwei Qi, Shuo Shao, Wei Gu, Tianhang Zheng, Puning Zhao, Zhan Qin, Kui Ren
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have exhibited remarkable capabilities but remain vulnerable to jailbreaking attacks, which can elicit harmful content from the models by manipulating the input prompts. Existing black-box jailbreaking techniques primarily rely on static prompts crafted with a single, non-adaptive strategy, or employ rigid combinations of several underperforming attack methods, which limits their adaptability and generalization. To address these limitations, we propose MAJIC, a Markovian adaptive jailbreaking framework that attacks black-box LLMs by iteratively combining diverse innovative disguise strategies. MAJIC first establishes a ``Disguise Strategy Pool'' by refining existing strategies and introducing several innovative approaches. To further improve the attack performance and efficiency, MAJIC formulate the sequential selection and fusion of strategies in the pool as a Markov chain. Under this formulation, MAJIC initializes and employs a Markov matrix to guide the strategy composition, where transition probabilities between strategies are dynamically adapted based on attack outcomes, thereby enabling MAJIC to learn and discover effective attack pathways tailored to the target model. Our empirical results demonstrate that MAJIC significantly outperforms existing jailbreak methods on prominent models such as GPT-4o and Gemini-2.0-flash, achieving over 90\% attack success rate with fewer than 15 queries per attempt on average.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the MAJIC paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MAJIC, a novel framework for jailbreaking Large Language Models (LLMs) under a black-box setting. MAJIC iteratively combines diverse "disguise strategies" to bypass LLM safety mechanisms. It employs a modular "Disguise Strategy Pool" of both refined existing techniques and new methods such as semantic inversion and literary disguise. The selection and combination of these strategies are modeled as a Markov chain, where a transition matrix guides the process. This matrix is initialized using a proxy LLM and dynamically updated during the attack based on a Q-learning inspired mechanism. Experiments on various state-of-the-art LLMs like Gemini, GPT-4, and Claude demonstrate that MAJIC achieves significantly higher attack success rates (ASR) with fewer queries compared to existing jailbreaking methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates significant novelty in several aspects:
    *   **Adaptive Strategy Composition:** Modeling the jailbreaking process as a Markov chain with dynamic updates is a novel approach that allows for adapting the attack strategy in real-time. This is a significant step beyond existing methods that rely on fixed or limited combinations of strategies.
    *   **Disguise Strategy Pool:**  The introduction and refinement of disguise strategies are novel. Furthermore, the inclusion of  "semantic inversion" and "literary disguise" techniques adds creativity to prompt manipulation, which helps in bypassing more complex safeguards.
    *   **Initialization of Markov Matrix:** Using proxy LLM and historical data to initialize the Markov transition matrix provides a good starting point for strategy selection. It leverages the pre-existing knowledge from weaker models to enhance the initial efficacy of the framework.

* **Significance:** The results present significant implications for LLM security:
    *   **Improved Attack Effectiveness:** MAJIC consistently outperforms existing methods in terms of ASR and query efficiency across various LLMs, even those renowned for their robust safety alignment (e.g., Claude-3.5-Sonnet).
    *   **Reduced Query Cost:** The ability to achieve high ASR with fewer queries is crucial in practical black-box settings where API usage can be limited and expensive.
    *   **Adaptive Attacks:** The dynamic nature of MAJIC is promising for countering evolving defenses and diverse model behaviors, addressing a significant limitation of current jailbreaking techniques.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper provides a thorough experimental evaluation of MAJIC on a diverse set of open and closed-source LLMs using established benchmarks.
    *   **Ablation Studies:**  Ablation studies are conducted to quantify the contribution of each component in the proposed framework like strategy pool, Markov model-based selection, and dynamic updates which strengthens the result.
    *   **Clear and Well-Structured:**  The paper is clearly written, and the methodology is well-explained with formal definitions.
    *   The paper includes additional analysis in the appendix.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Auxiliary LLMs:** The framework relies on auxiliary models for initializing the transition matrix and selecting prompts, which is potentially costly. Although the runtime cost is less, the resources for initializing Markov Matrix cannot be ignored.
    *   **Limited Scope of Disguise Strategies:** The effectiveness of MAJIC is inherently tied to the disguise strategies included in the pool. It could be more efficient to provide the means to scale or increase the number of such disguise strategies in real-time as a new attack is attempted. The disguise strategy pool expansion mechanism could be further analyzed to see the impact of attack performance.

* **Potential Influence:**
    *   MAJIC's adaptive approach could influence the design of future jailbreaking attacks, shifting the focus from static prompts to dynamic and feedback-driven strategies.
    *   The framework could be adapted as a red-teaming tool for evaluating and strengthening the safety of LLMs during development.

**Score: 8.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper introduces a novel and effective framework for jailbreaking LLMs with significant improvements in ASR and query efficiency. The adaptive strategy composition using a Markov chain and dynamic updates is a compelling contribution. The weaknesses primarily involve the reliance on auxiliary models and limited disguise strategies. However, the strengths in evaluation, clarity, and potential influence outweigh these limitations. The paper presents a significant advancement in the field of LLM security, demonstrating the ongoing challenges in achieving robust safety alignment.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Doğal Dil İşlemede Tokenizasyon Standartları ve Ölçümü: Türkçe Üzerinden Büyük Dil Modellerinin Karşılaştırmalı Analizi
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13058v1)
- **Authors**: M. Ali Bayram, Ali Arda Fincan, Ahmet Semih Gümüş, Sercan Karakaş, Banu Diri, Savaş Yıldırım
- **Abstract**: Tokenization is a fundamental preprocessing step in Natural Language Processing (NLP), significantly impacting the capability of large language models (LLMs) to capture linguistic and semantic nuances. This study introduces a novel evaluation framework addressing tokenization challenges specific to morphologically-rich and low-resource languages such as Turkish. Utilizing the Turkish MMLU (TR-MMLU) dataset, comprising 6,200 multiple-choice questions from the Turkish education system, we assessed tokenizers based on vocabulary size, token count, processing time, language-specific token percentages (\%TR), and token purity (\%Pure). These newly proposed metrics measure how effectively tokenizers preserve linguistic structures. Our analysis reveals that language-specific token percentages exhibit a stronger correlation with downstream performance (e.g., MMLU scores) than token purity. Furthermore, increasing model parameters alone does not necessarily enhance linguistic performance, underscoring the importance of tailored, language-specific tokenization methods. The proposed framework establishes robust and practical tokenization standards for morphologically complex languages.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of tokenization strategies on the performance of large language models (LLMs) when applied to Turkish, a morphologically rich and low-resource language. It proposes a novel evaluation framework centered around metrics like language-specific token percentage (%TR) and token purity (% Pure), which aim to assess how effectively tokenizers preserve linguistic structures. Using the TR-MMLU dataset, the study compares several tokenizers based on vocabulary size, token count, processing time, %TR, and % Pure. The findings suggest that a higher %TR correlates strongly with better MMLU scores, indicating the importance of language-specific tokenization. The paper argues that simply increasing model parameters isn't sufficient for linguistic performance and that tailored tokenization methods are crucial for morphologically complex languages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its focus on Turkish and the introduction of the %TR and % Pure metrics for evaluating tokenization. While the general idea of language-specific tokenization isn't entirely new, the specific application to Turkish and the proposed metrics provide a concrete framework for assessing and comparing tokenization methods in this context. The use of TR-MMLU is also a plus, grounding the evaluation in a realistic and challenging dataset.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is moderate.  It highlights a crucial aspect of NLP for morphologically rich languages, which often get less attention in research dominated by English. By demonstrating the importance of language-specific tokenization for Turkish LLMs, it contributes to the ongoing effort to improve NLP models for diverse languages. The proposed framework can be adapted for other similar languages. The analysis using a complex dataset such as TR-MMLU adds value for researchers to use in future works.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly identifies the challenges of tokenization for morphologically rich languages.
    *   **Well-Defined Metrics:** The %TR and % Pure metrics are well-defined and provide a quantitative way to assess tokenization quality.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study evaluates several tokenizers across multiple metrics, providing a holistic view of their performance.
    *   **Practical Recommendations:**  The paper concludes with practical recommendations for developing better tokenization strategies for morphologically complex languages.
    * **Reproducibility**: The study releases all models, dataset and code which enhances reproducibility and allows other researchers to build upon their works.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:**  The study is focused solely on Turkish. While the framework is generalizable, the specific findings might not directly translate to all morphologically rich languages.
    *   **Metric Limitations:** The %TR and % Pure metrics, while useful, might not capture all aspects of tokenization quality. For instance, they don't directly measure the semantic coherence of tokenized sequences. The metrics are based on ITU Turkis NLP Web Service and Kalbur library. The quality of this library has not been benchmarked and it may include limitations.
    *   **Model Selection**: While the paper compares different tokenizers, it doesn't explore the impact of combining different tokenizer approaches on the performance of LLMs. Furthermore, the paper could be strengthened by providing a discussion on the impact of dataset size on the performance.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper could influence future research by:

    *   Encouraging the development of more sophisticated tokenization methods for morphologically rich languages.
    *   Providing a standardized evaluation framework for comparing tokenization strategies.
    *   Raising awareness of the importance of language-specific tokenization in the NLP community.

**Rigorous Rationale:**
The paper's strength lies in its concrete application to Turkish and the proposal of specific metrics. However, the limited scope (single language) and potential limitations of the metrics prevent it from being a groundbreaking contribution. The study confirms existing knowledge by proving the effect of high parameter counts and special-purpose tokens. More detailed analysis of specific Turkish linguistic structures (e.g. suffixes, cases) on specific models would add to the significance. The conclusions are reasonable and supported by the experiments. The reproducibility is high thanks to open source release of resources.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Reinforced Context Order Recovery for Adaptive Reasoning and Planning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13070v1)
- **Authors**: Long Ma, Fangwei Zhong, Yizhou Wang
- **Abstract**: Modern causal language models, followed by rapid developments in discrete diffusion models, can now produce a wide variety of interesting and useful content. However, these families of models are predominantly trained to output tokens with a fixed (left-to-right) or random order, which may deviate from the logical order in which tokens are generated originally. In this paper, we observe that current causal and diffusion models encounter difficulties in problems that require adaptive token generation orders to solve tractably, which we characterize with the $\mathcal{V}$-information framework. Motivated by this, we propose Reinforced Context Order Recovery (ReCOR), a reinforcement-learning-based framework to extract adaptive, data-dependent token generation orders from text data without annotations. Self-supervised by token prediction statistics, ReCOR estimates the hardness of predicting every unfilled token and adaptively selects the next token during both training and inference. Experiments on challenging reasoning and planning datasets demonstrate the superior performance of ReCOR compared with baselines, sometimes outperforming oracle models supervised with the ground-truth order.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Reinforced Context Order Recovery (ReCOR), a reinforcement learning framework designed to address the limitations of causal language models (CLMs) and diffusion models when tackling reasoning and planning tasks that necessitate adaptive token generation orders. ReCOR learns to predict the optimal token generation order without explicit order annotations, using token prediction statistics as self-supervision. It casts order prediction as a decision-making problem and jointly optimizes a token prediction model and an order prediction policy. Experiments on arithmetic and logic puzzle datasets demonstrate ReCOR's superior performance compared to baselines, even outperforming oracle models with ground-truth order access.  The paper highlights the benefits of training with adaptive orders, contrasting it with methods that only apply adaptive strategies during inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using reinforcement learning to learn an adaptive token ordering for text generation is relatively novel, particularly in the context of overcoming limitations of CLMs and diffusion models in reasoning tasks.  While some previous works have explored adaptive token ordering, they often rely on explicit supervision or are limited to inference-time adjustments. ReCOR distinguishes itself by learning the order *during training* in a self-supervised manner. The application of predictive V-information to formalize token hardness, though building on prior work, provides a theoretical justification for the approach.
* **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in addressing a fundamental limitation of current language models in complex reasoning and planning scenarios. By allowing models to adaptively choose the order in which they generate tokens, ReCOR enables them to tackle problems that are intractable for models with fixed generation orders. The experimental results on arithmetic and logic puzzles demonstrate a clear improvement in performance, suggesting the potential for wider applicability.  The analysis of why inference-only adaptive methods can fail is also a significant contribution.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of fixed token order in reasoning tasks and proposes a compelling solution.
    * **Self-Supervised Learning:** The self-supervised nature of the approach is a significant strength, as it avoids the need for costly and often unavailable order annotations.
    * **Rigorous Evaluation:** The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, including comparisons to strong baselines and ablation studies. The comparison with oracle models and the analysis of different reward functions are particularly valuable.
    * **Theoretical Justification:** The use of predictive V-information provides a theoretical framework for understanding the problem and motivating the proposed solution.
    * **Careful Analysis:**  The comparison of ReCOR with methods such as AdaMDM, and the analysis of when adaptive orders are needed, significantly strengthens the paper.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Computational Cost:** RL-based training can be computationally expensive, and the paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the computational resources required for ReCOR compared to baselines. While the paper mentions using KV-caching during inference and compares FLOPS, a deeper exploration of training cost scaling would be useful.
    * **Limited Scope of Evaluation:**  While the experiments are convincing, the datasets are relatively constrained. Demonstrating the effectiveness of ReCOR on a broader range of reasoning and planning tasks would further enhance the impact of the work. Applying ReCOR on real-world complex tasks such as program synthesis, knowledge graph reasoning, or planning with external tools/API calls would also strengthen the paper's applicability.
    * **Complexity of the Framework:** ReCOR introduces a more complex training pipeline with RL and necessitates some fine-tuning. It would be useful to include a deeper analysis on the sensitivity of ReCOR's performance to various hyperparameters, which can help potential users to more effectively apply the proposed method.
    * **Modest Novelty:** While relatively novel, the idea of using reinforcement learning in NLP is not new; previous work has explored using RL for other aspects of text generation.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 7. The paper presents a novel and significant approach to addressing the limitations of CLMs and diffusion models in reasoning tasks. The self-supervised learning framework, the use of predictive V-information, and the comprehensive experimental evaluation are all strong points. However, the computational cost and the limited scope of evaluation slightly temper the overall impact. While ReCOR addresses a key limitation in certain kinds of problems, a broader real-world and computational validation and analysis will greatly enhance its practicality and impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Transthoracic to Transesophageal: Cross-Modality Generation using LoRA Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13077v1)
- **Authors**: Emmanuel Oladokun, Yuxuan Ou, Anna Novikova, Daria Kulikova, Sarina Thomas, Jurica Šprem, Vicente Grau
- **Abstract**: Deep diffusion models excel at realistic image synthesis but demand large training sets-an obstacle in data-scarce domains like transesophageal echocardiography (TEE). While synthetic augmentation has boosted performance in transthoracic echo (TTE), TEE remains critically underrepresented, limiting the reach of deep learning in this high-impact modality. We address this gap by adapting a TTE-trained, mask-conditioned diffusion backbone to TEE with only a limited number of new cases and adapters as small as $10^5$ parameters. Our pipeline combines Low-Rank Adaptation with MaskR$^2$, a lightweight remapping layer that aligns novel mask formats with the pretrained model's conditioning channels. This design lets users adapt models to new datasets with a different set of anatomical structures to the base model's original set. Through a targeted adaptation strategy, we find that adapting only MLP layers suffices for high-fidelity TEE synthesis. Finally, mixing less than 200 real TEE frames with our synthetic echoes improves the dice score on a multiclass segmentation task, particularly boosting performance on underrepresented right-heart structures. Our results demonstrate that (1) semantically controlled TEE images can be generated with low overhead, (2) MaskR$^2$ effectively transforms unseen mask formats into compatible formats without damaging downstream task performance, and (3) our method generates images that are effective for improving performance on a downstream task of multiclass segmentation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the scarcity of data in transesophageal echocardiography (TEE) by adapting a diffusion model pre-trained on the more readily available transthoracic echo (TTE) data. The authors introduce a LoRA-based method for efficient fine-tuning with limited TEE data, a targeted adaptation strategy to identify key layers for adaptation, and a "MaskR2" scheme to transform semantic maps to match the pre-trained model's input format.  They demonstrate that this approach can generate realistic synthetic TEE images that improve downstream multiclass segmentation performance, particularly for underrepresented right-heart structures.  The key contributions are the low-overhead adaptation process, the mask adaptation technique, and the demonstrable benefit of the generated images for downstream tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of adapting a diffusion model from one echocardiography modality (TTE) to another (TEE) using LoRA is incremental, but the specific implementation with MaskR2 and targeted adaptation shows some degree of innovation.  Diffusion models and LoRA have been applied in medical imaging before, but the specific adaptation strategy, including the observation that MLP layers are sufficient for high-fidelity TEE synthesis, is novel. The MaskR2 technique, while simple in its component operations, provides a practical solution to a common problem in cross-dataset adaptation. The novelty mainly lies in the specific combination of techniques and their application to the TTE-to-TEE domain.
* **Significance:** TEE data scarcity is a recognized bottleneck in applying deep learning in cardiology. The paper's demonstration that high-quality synthetic data can be generated with minimal TEE cases has practical significance. The MaskR2 component also has the potential to simplify adaptation to new anatomical structures or mask definitions.  The improvement in multiclass segmentation, especially for the often-underrepresented right heart structures, is a valuable result, highlighting the potential for improved diagnostic tools.  However, the significance is limited by the internal nature of one of the datasets, which restricts reproducibility.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Practical Solution:** Provides a practical solution to a real-world problem (TEE data scarcity).
    * **Efficient Adaptation:** Demonstrates the efficiency of LoRA in adapting a large model with minimal computational overhead.
    * **MaskR2 contribution:** Addresses a common challenge in transfer learning with semantic maps.
    * **Downstream Task Validation:**  Provides strong evidence that the generated data are useful for a downstream task.
    * **Clear Methodology:** The paper clearly outlines the methodology and experimental setup.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Innovation:**  Builds upon existing techniques (diffusion models, LoRA) with modest modifications.
    * **Dataset limitations:**  The reliance on an internal dataset limits reproducibility and wider impact.  While CAMUS is used for pre-training, the actual adaptation leverages a private dataset.
    * **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is focused on a specific multiclass segmentation task.  Wider validation (e.g., performance on other diagnostic tasks, clinical applicability) is missing.
    * **Image Quality Metrics:** Acknowledged limitations of FID and LPIPS for medical echo images are a concern. Using task-specific metrics (segmentation) is a better approach, but subjective assessment could also be useful to complement the objective measures.
    * **Limited Ablation Studies:** While the paper explores layer selection for LoRA, further ablation studies (e.g., exploring different MaskR2 strategies) would be beneficial.
* **Potential Influence:**  The paper could encourage further research on data augmentation techniques for specialized medical imaging modalities using pre-trained models. MaskR2 could become a useful tool for adapting models to new datasets with differing annotation schemes.  The observation about the importance of MLPs for segmentation is interesting and warrants further investigation.

**Overall:** The paper offers a valuable contribution to the field by providing a practical and efficient method for generating synthetic TEE data. However, the incremental nature of the innovation, the dataset limitations, and the limited scope of the validation prevent a higher score.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 6/10

### DMS:Diffusion-Based Multi-Baseline Stereo Generation for Improving Self-Supervised Depth Estimation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13091v1)
- **Authors**: Zihua Liu, Yizhou Li, Songyan Zhang, Masatoshi Okutomi
- **Abstract**: While supervised stereo matching and monocular depth estimation have advanced significantly with learning-based algorithms, self-supervised methods using stereo images as supervision signals have received relatively less focus and require further investigation. A primary challenge arises from ambiguity introduced during photometric reconstruction, particularly due to missing corresponding pixels in ill-posed regions of the target view, such as occlusions and out-of-frame areas. To address this and establish explicit photometric correspondences, we propose DMS, a model-agnostic approach that utilizes geometric priors from diffusion models to synthesize novel views along the epipolar direction, guided by directional prompts. Specifically, we finetune a Stable Diffusion model to simulate perspectives at key positions: left-left view shifted from the left camera, right-right view shifted from the right camera, along with an additional novel view between the left and right cameras. These synthesized views supplement occluded pixels, enabling explicit photometric reconstruction. Our proposed DMS is a cost-free, ''plug-and-play'' method that seamlessly enhances self-supervised stereo matching and monocular depth estimation, and relies solely on unlabeled stereo image pairs for both training and synthesizing. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach, with up to 35% outlier reduction and state-of-the-art performance across multiple benchmark datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DMS (Diffusion-based Multi-Baseline Stereo Generation), a novel approach to enhance self-supervised depth estimation using stereo images. DMS leverages geometric priors from diffusion models (specifically, a fine-tuned Stable Diffusion model) to synthesize novel views along the epipolar line, guided by directional text prompts. This process generates supplementary views (left-left, right-right, and an intermediate view) that fill in occluded or out-of-frame pixels, enabling more explicit photometric correspondences and improved depth estimation. The method is model-agnostic and cost-free, relying only on unlabeled stereo image pairs for training and synthesis. Experiments show that DMS improves self-supervised stereo matching and monocular depth estimation, achieving state-of-the-art performance with reduced outliers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in *effectively using a diffusion model for multi-baseline stereo image generation in a self-supervised depth estimation context*.  While diffusion models have been used for image generation and even multi-view synthesis before, the targeted application and the approach of using directional prompts to control view generation along the epipolar line are significant.  Using a fine-tuned Stable Diffusion model to generate *intermediate* viewpoints represents a creative application of a pre-trained model and an interesting insight into the model's learned geometric priors. The *plug-and-play nature* adds practical value.
*   **Significance:** The problem of ambiguity in photometric reconstruction due to occlusions and out-of-frame regions is a well-known limitation in self-supervised stereo depth estimation. DMS directly addresses this by providing additional matching cues. The performance gains demonstrated across various datasets (particularly the outlier reduction) suggest a significant practical improvement over existing methods. By reducing the reliance on context propagation, DMS might lead to more robust and reliable depth estimation, a critical requirement for applications like autonomous driving.  The ability to extend stereo baselines *without* requiring additional multi-view training data is also impactful.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Effective use of diffusion models:** DMS cleverly leverages the geometric understanding learned by a large pre-trained diffusion model.
    *   **Improved outlier reduction:**  The experiments clearly demonstrate a substantial reduction in outliers, which is a crucial metric for depth estimation accuracy.
    *   **Model-agnostic and cost-effective:** The plug-and-play nature of DMS means that it can be easily integrated into existing self-supervised depth estimation pipelines without significant overhead. It removes the need for a complex physical multi-camera setup or manually labeled data for multi-baseline training.
    *   **Strong experimental results:** The paper presents comprehensive experimental results on several benchmark datasets, showing state-of-the-art performance compared to existing methods.
    *   **Intermediate view generation:** The ability to control the shift distance through rescaling offers more robust supervision signals.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Stable Diffusion:** The approach is tied to the performance and limitations of the underlying Stable Diffusion model. Artifacts or biases in the diffusion model could potentially propagate into the generated stereo images, potentially affecting the final depth estimation accuracy. It's important to remember that Stable Diffusion and similar models can exhibit biases, so it is good practice to perform responsible AI evaluations.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper states DMS is cost-free. While it doesn't require additional labeled data, the generation of multi-baseline images through diffusion models *is* computationally intensive. The inference time information is provided, and must be considered.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** While ablation studies are presented, more could have been done to isolate the effect of each synthesized view (left-left, right-right, center) and different prompt combinations.
    *   **Limited qualitative results:** While some visualization is presented, deeper qualitative analysis regarding failure cases or specific regions of improvement would strengthen the paper.  For instance, are there specific scene characteristics where DMS struggles?
    *   **Dependency on accurate prompts:** The prompt engineering is relatively simple (using "to left" or "to right"). If the Stable Diffusion model's behavior is significantly altered, for instance, with new versions or different finetuning datasets, might the directional prompting need adjustment?

*   **Potential Influence:** DMS has the potential to significantly influence the field of self-supervised depth estimation.  It offers a practical way to improve accuracy and robustness by extending stereo baselines using the power of diffusion models.  Future research could explore using more sophisticated prompting techniques, exploring other generative models (e.g., 3D-aware GANs), and adapting DMS to other self-supervised learning tasks. It could open new research avenues related to incorporating and utilizing knowledge from large pre-trained vision-language models within traditional computer vision tasks.

**Score:** 8

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel, significant, and practical contribution to self-supervised depth estimation. The clever use of diffusion models to generate multi-baseline views effectively addresses the long-standing problem of occlusions and out-of-frame regions, leading to substantial performance improvements. The model-agnostic and cost-free nature of DMS makes it a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners. While the method has some limitations (reliance on diffusion models, computational cost), its strengths outweigh its weaknesses. It is a well-written paper with comprehensive experimental results and a clear demonstration of its effectiveness and potential. The impact on the field is likely to be high, prompting further research into integrating large pre-trained models to enhance geometric reasoning in self-supervised learning.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### VerilogLAVD: LLM-Aided Rule Generation for Vulnerability Detection in Verilog
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13092v1)
- **Authors**: Xiang Long, Yingjie Xia, Xiyuan Chen, Li Kuang
- **Abstract**: Timely detection of hardware vulnerabilities during the early design stage is critical for reducing remediation costs. Existing early detection techniques often require specialized security expertise, limiting their usability. Recent efforts have explored the use of large language models (LLMs) for Verilog vulnerability detection. However, LLMs struggle to capture the structure in Verilog code, resulting in inconsistent detection results. To this end, we propose VerilogLAVD, the first LLM-aided graph traversal rule generation approach for Verilog vulnerability detection. Our approach introduces the Verilog Property Graph (VeriPG), a unified representation of Verilog code. It combines syntactic features extracted from the abstract syntax tree (AST) with semantic information derived from control flow and data dependency graphs. We leverage LLMs to generate VeriPG-based detection rules from Common Weakness Enumeration (CWE) descriptions. These rules guide the rule executor that traversal VeriPG for potential vulnerabilities. To evaluate VerilogLAVD, we build a dataset collected from open-source repositories and synthesized data. In our empirical evaluation on 77 Verilog designs encompassing 12 CWE types, VerilogLAVD achieves an F1-score of 0.54. Compared to the LLM-only and LLM with external knowledge baselines, VerilogLAVD improves F1-score by 0.31 and 0.27, respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "VerilogLAVD: LLM-Aided Rule Generation for Vulnerability Detection in Verilog" proposes a novel approach for detecting hardware vulnerabilities in Verilog code using Large Language Models (LLMs). The core idea is to generate Verilog Property Graph (VeriPG) traversal rules from Common Weakness Enumeration (CWE) descriptions. VeriPG combines syntactic and semantic information from the AST, CFG, and DDG of Verilog code. The authors also introduce a validation mechanism to improve the correctness of the generated rules and a specialized rule executor for efficient traversal of the VeriPG. Experimental results on a custom dataset show that VerilogLAVD outperforms LLM-only and LLM with external knowledge baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a combination of ideas that contribute to novelty, especially in the hardware security domain. The VeriPG is a significant contribution, because it provides a consolidated data structure for analyzing Verilog code in a way more suitable for vulnerability detection than simply using ASTs.  The validation-based rule generation addresses a key limitation of using LLMs directly, which is their tendency to produce incorrect or inaccurate outputs. The specialized rule executor, designed explicitly for VeriPG and Verilog semantics, is a useful enhancement. The primary novelty lies in the specific combination of LLM-based rule generation with graph-based code representation and validation specifically tailored for Verilog.
*   **Significance:** Timely vulnerability detection in hardware design is crucial, and the paper tackles this problem by leveraging LLMs to automate rule generation. If the VerilogLAVD improves usability for engineers with less security expertise, the significance is increased. The experimental results suggest promising performance, making it a valuable step towards more accessible hardware security analysis. A key contribution is improving automation and reducing the reliance on specialized security expertise.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly outlines the limitations of existing approaches and the need for automated vulnerability detection in early design stages.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** The proposed VerilogLAVD approach is well-defined and the components (VeriPG, rule generation, validation, and executor) are logically connected.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The authors perform experiments on a custom dataset and compare against reasonable baselines.  The performance improvements demonstrate the potential of their approach.
    *   **Addresses LLM Limitations:** The rule validation component addresses the issue of LLM generating incorrect results.
    *   **Strong Organization:** The paper is well-organized and easy to follow.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Limitations:** The dataset is custom-built, although inspired by a real-world open source repository. The size and representativeness of the dataset could be questioned, which limits the generalizability of the results.  A demonstration that it accurately handles real-world hardware designs from diverse sources is lacking.
    *   **Limited Baseline Comparison:** The selection of baselines (LLM-only, LLM+Knowledge) is valid. Comparing against RTL security analysis tools (e.g., Spyglass) would have been stronger, although they concede that those tools primarily target functional correctness, which is a slightly different scope. A direct comparison against other tools used for security rule generation would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:** Although the evaluation includes some efficiency measurements, there are lingering questions on performance for very large Verilog designs. The rule executor, while specialized, may still face scalability challenges with significantly larger designs. The experiment only included single-module Verilog code and varying lines of code.
    *   **Rule Complexity:** The paper alludes to more complicated rules.  A full analysis of the complexity and potential limitations on the type of rules/vulnerabilities this approach can detect should be described in greater depth. It also needs to be expanded on if the LLM can't generate code to properly operate primitive traversal.
*   **Impact:** If VerilogLAVD can be developed into a robust and scalable tool, it could significantly impact the hardware security field by making vulnerability detection more accessible to a wider range of engineers. It paves the way for better integration of security into the hardware design process at an earlier stage.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-defined approach to an important problem. The VeriPG is a valuable contribution, and the experimental results show encouraging performance improvements. The work addresses key challenges associated with directly using LLMs for code analysis, such as accuracy and consistency. The weaknesses mainly revolve around the evaluation dataset's limitations and scalability concerns.  Despite these limitations, the paper makes a significant contribution and represents a positive step towards automating vulnerability detection in Verilog designs. Further development with more diverse and large datasets and more rigorous evaluation would increase confidence in the practicality and impact of VerilogLAVD.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Denoising diffusion models for inverse design of inflatable structures with programmable deformations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13097v1)
- **Authors**: Sara Karimi, Nikolaos N. Vlassis
- **Abstract**: Programmable structures are systems whose undeformed geometries and material property distributions are deliberately designed to achieve prescribed deformed configurations under specific loading conditions. Inflatable structures are a prominent example, using internal pressurization to realize large, nonlinear deformations in applications ranging from soft robotics and deployable aerospace systems to biomedical devices and adaptive architecture. We present a generative design framework based on denoising diffusion probabilistic models (DDPMs) for the inverse design of elastic structures undergoing large, nonlinear deformations under pressure-driven actuation. The method formulates the inverse design as a conditional generation task, using geometric descriptors of target deformed states as inputs and outputting image-based representations of the undeformed configuration.
